# Claim-level evidence ledger

Generated from researcher ledgers. Confidence concerns the bounded statement; causal status is separate. No claim is certified solely by inclusion here.

## AGR-K01

Erickson's northern Titicaca investigation documents an engineered raised-field landscape and reconstructed blocks with warmer, shorter frost exposure on two reported frost nights; it does not measure an ancient basin-wide frost advantage.

**Cases:** AGR-C01. **Evidence:** archaeological. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [AGR-S01: Prehistoric Landscape Management in the Andean Highlands: Raised Field Agriculture and its Environmental Impact](https://anthropology.sas.upenn.edu/sites/www.sas.upenn.edu.anthropology/files/page/EricksonPrehistoricLandscapeManagementAndeanRaisedField%26EnvironImpact1992b.pdf), pp. 289–297; chronology p. 290, hydrology pp. 293–294, microclimate p. 296. Supports: Excavation, rebuilding sequences, local raised-field functions and frost observations in experimental blocks.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Raised surfaces and adjacent canals alter drainage, water availability and heat exchange -> crops experience a changed local environment -> possible protection on frost nights.
- **strongest rival:** Plot position, moisture or crop differences rather than the raised-field configuration could explain the observed temperature contrast.
- **discriminating observation:** Repeated matched raised and flat plots with equivalent crops and soil, measured through frost and non-frost seasons, would separate configuration from location effects.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S01 was inspected for hydrological explanation and actual temperature evidence; two nights of warmer/shorter frost exposure were reported. A full multiyear matched trial was not found in the inspected article.
- **remaining ambiguity:** The engineering mechanism is plausible and locally observed; seasonal average protection, yield consequences and ancient operating conditions remain unmeasured here.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Two nights and small blocks are not average seasonal conditions; different field designs, water levels, soils and crop varieties can alter effects.

**Inference limit:** Northern-basin early use is approximately three millennia before the 1992 study, not a precise invention date. Excavated persistence does not reveal whether all surviving fields operated simultaneously.

## AGR-K02

Bandy's reassessment reports abandonment in specified 1980s–1990s raised-field rehabilitation programmes while also recording reconstruction elsewhere through 2000; the evidence does not supply a basin-wide abandonment rate.

**Cases:** AGR-C01. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [AGR-S02: Energetic efficiency and political expediency in Titicaca Basin raised field agriculture](https://www.academia.edu/6455173/Energetic_Efficiency_and_Political_Expediency_in_Titicaca_Basin_Raised_Field_Agriculture), pp. 273–275. Supports: Contrasts revival claims, Swartley's reported revisits and continuing reconstruction totals.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** This records the bounded rehabilitation and abandonment evidence without attributing its cause or estimating prevalence.

**Counterevidence/rival:** The same account contains continuing construction and heterogeneous project experiences; later persistence was not exhaustively surveyed.

**Inference limit:** Swartley's underlying dissertation/book and the programme registers were not acquired. Her 1996–1997 revisit is inherited evidence about observed fields, not a census of all Titicaca communities.

## AGR-K03

Bandy challenges claims of universally superior raised-field labour productivity by incorporating crop rotation, fallow and maintenance labour; whether raised fields outperform dryland alternatives depends on the comparison and accounting assumptions.

**Cases:** AGR-C01. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** contested.

- [AGR-S02: Energetic efficiency and political expediency in Titicaca Basin raised field agriculture](https://www.academia.edu/6455173/Energetic_Efficiency_and_Political_Expediency_in_Titicaca_Basin_Raised_Field_Agriculture), pp. 280–288. Supports: Energy/labour accounting, rotation comparisons, heterogeneous reported yields and political-economic interpretations.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Construction and recurrent maintenance plus rotation/fallow change the labour denominator -> initial high harvests can overstate sustained food per workday -> revival may be less attractive than existing rotations.
- **strongest rival:** Raised fields may give sufficiently reliable output per scarce hectare or shift peak-season work enough to compensate for lower average labour efficiency; tenure or organisational failure may explain abandonment instead.
- **discriminating observation:** Matched multiyear rotations including rebuilding, seed quality, crop prices and peak-season opportunity costs; durable non-use despite verified private superiority would favour institutional or changing-livelihood explanations.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S02 explicitly compares full-rotation and maintenance assumptions and reports heterogeneous studies. The underlying five-season Fernández Valdivia–García Chire study was not acquired.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Yield and labour estimates share earlier studies, and definitions of maintenance and opportunity cost differ. The account cannot identify the motive of every ancient or modern cultivator.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Some cited comparisons favour raised fields. Food per hectare, water control or timing may matter more than food per workday where suitable land is scarce.

**Inference limit:** A schematic reassessment, not a new controlled multiseason trial. Ancient political mobilisation and modern programme abandonment are distinct explanations, neither proved by the labour calculation alone.

## AGR-K04

In the Oos–Petanu model and associated field account, rice-calendar coordination mediates a tradeoff between pest suppression through synchronised fallow and competing irrigation-water demands.

**Cases:** AGR-C02. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [AGR-S03: Emergent Properties of Balinese Water Temple Networks: Coadaptation on a Rugged Fitness Landscape](https://faculty.sites.iastate.edu/tesfatsi/archive/tesfatsi/LansingKremer.BaliWaterTemples.pdf), pp. 100–103 and 106–111. Supports: The 172-subak setting, crop/water observations and model of pest–water interactions.
- [AGR-S04: Coordination in irrigation systems: An analysis of the Lansing–Kremer model of Bali](https://dlc.dlib.indiana.edu/dlc/bitstreams/34db1e42-4d4f-4b25-a7d7-16e8f023677a/download), proof sections 2–3; pp. 19–20. Supports: Reimplementation and sensitivity of coordination outcomes to ecological and decision assumptions.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Connected fields share water and pest habitat -> individual planting choices affect neighbours -> joint calendars can reduce some external costs while balancing water peaks.
- **strongest rival:** Independent schedules could perform similarly under weaker pest movement or abundant water; observed calendars might be sustained principally by social authority rather than yield optimisation.
- **discriminating observation:** Compare predictions under changed pest/water parameters and observe matched ecological settings using alternative coordination arrangements; independent scheduling performing equally well where the model predicts losses would weaken its mechanism.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S03 provides ecological model and field calibration; AGR-S04 reproduces the original result but finds sensitivity to assumptions and identifies alternatives to synchronisation.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Simulation behaviour is not direct proof that ecological optimisation caused institutional emergence or continuation; enforcement costs and social objectives are not fully identified.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** The strength and preferred form of coordination change when pest pressure, water scarcity or the decision rule changes; watershed output is not identical to every subak's gain.

**Inference limit:** Model-supported local mechanism, not identified historical origin or universal optimality of temples. Janssen independently reanalyses the same model rather than supplying an independent field experiment.

## AGR-K05

The documented 1988–1989 Manuaba calendar combined temple-linked coordination with high-yielding rice varieties, including IR64, so restoration of coordination did not require wholesale rejection of improved seed.

**Cases:** AGR-C02. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [AGR-S03: Emergent Properties of Balinese Water Temple Networks: Coadaptation on a Rugged Fitness Landscape](https://faculty.sites.iastate.edu/tesfatsi/archive/tesfatsi/LansingKremer.BaliWaterTemples.pdf), pp. 100–102. Supports: Named varieties and coordinated calendar in the observed system.
- [AGR-S04: Coordination in irrigation systems: An analysis of the Lansing–Kremer model of Bali](https://dlc.dlib.indiana.edu/dlc/bitstreams/34db1e42-4d4f-4b25-a7d7-16e8f023677a/download), proof pp. 2–3. Supports: Historical account of disruption and restoration of coordinated irrigation practices.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** The evidence establishes a coexisting seed–institution configuration, not an isolated causal estimate of restoration.

**Counterevidence/rival:** The historical disruption account partly inherits Lansing's earlier research; the observed mixed configuration does not establish its yield advantage over every alternative.

**Inference limit:** Specific calendar and interval, not a measured Bali-wide adoption rate or proof that every promoted variety was retained.

## AGR-K06

Janssen's model reanalysis makes the claimed coordination advantage conditional on pest dynamics and farmers' decision rules, and distinguishes synchronisation from other coordinated schedules.

**Cases:** AGR-C02. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** contested.

- [AGR-S04: Coordination in irrigation systems: An analysis of the Lansing–Kremer model of Bali](https://dlc.dlib.indiana.edu/dlc/bitstreams/34db1e42-4d4f-4b25-a7d7-16e8f023677a/download), proof sections 3 and 5, especially pp. 19–20. Supports: Sensitivity analysis, optimisation alternatives and distribution of watershed gains.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Changing pest response or imitation rules changes the simulated payoff surface -> locally adaptive schedules change -> original synchronisation/optimality result is conditional.
- **strongest rival:** If original assumptions closely describe the actual watershed, the sensitivity cases may be less empirically relevant even though mathematically valid.
- **discriminating observation:** Measure pest growth, movement, water scarcity and actual farmer updating independently; compare out-of-sample predictions from the competing model variants.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S04 reimplemented the model, recovered its central result and changed key assumptions; the proof explicitly discusses optimisation and coordination alternatives.
- **remaining ambiguity:** This contribution does not re-run source code or estimate the best-fitting ecological model. Computational sensitivity bounds the claim but does not settle historical causal importance.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** The central Lansing–Kremer result can be reproduced under its original assumptions; model sensitivity does not demonstrate that historical cooperation was ineffective.

**Inference limit:** An independent computational critique of a shared model, not a second field dataset. It does not select the best real institution without measurements of ecological parameters and implementation costs.

## AGR-K07

Carney documents Jahaly-Pacharr land registered to women being reclassified as household rice fields under male control; nominal allocation and the irrigation package did not fix who controlled output and labour.

**Cases:** AGR-C03. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [AGR-S05: Contract Farming and Female Rice Growers in The Gambia](https://media.odi.org/documents/8169.pdf), sections 3–5, pp. 4–10. Supports: Pre-project rights, project allocation, reclassification and household labour obligations.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Project rules allocate irrigated plots -> local household authority reclassifies control -> women may supply labour without equivalent control of crop returns.
- **strongest rival:** Input/credit obligations or profitable household specialisation could change management without entitlement loss being the main reason for labour responses.
- **discriminating observation:** Track the same plots and individuals through allocation, output control and labour decisions; compare credible rights or compensation changes while holding technical inputs and project obligations constant.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S05 describes allocation and local reclassification and contrasts communities' practical rights. No randomised or independently timed rights change was found.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Political status, crop alternatives and family obligations also differ between communities; the rights pathway is supported but not separately identified.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Rights and bargaining differed among communities and households; later participation cannot be reduced to a single invariant gender rule.

**Inference limit:** Field-based institutional interpretation, not randomised ownership or a universal account of all Gambian rice. Registration alone is not measured as an exogenous treatment.

## AGR-K08

Carney links women's withdrawal from uncompensated project labour and their use of paid labour groups to control of returns and available crop alternatives, while documenting concurrent repair, input-price, diesel and marketing problems.

**Cases:** AGR-C03. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [AGR-S05: Contract Farming and Female Rice Growers in The Gambia](https://media.odi.org/documents/8169.pdf), sections 6–8, pp. 11–18. Supports: Negotiation, labour resistance, wage groups, crop alternatives and later technical/economic difficulties.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Uncompensated obligations reduce a cultivator's private return relative to independent plots or wages -> negotiation or withdrawal changes labour supplied to irrigated rice.
- **strongest rival:** Machinery, fuel, credit and marketing failures could lower returns for everyone and explain declining effort without a distinct control mechanism.
- **discriminating observation:** Participation changing after enforceable crop shares or wages, with operating reliability and prices held comparable, would discriminate control from general project failure.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S05 reports specific negotiation/withdrawal and paid-group adaptations, alongside several operating and price changes rather than treating all decline as labour resistance.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Institutional and operational changes overlap. Different outside options partly track ethnicity and political connections; their separate effects remain unresolved.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** A fall in project yields can reflect rational reallocation toward rainfed crops or non-labour operating failures; withdrawal was not universal.

**Inference limit:** Differences across places and groups are informative but not random assignments. This does not estimate labour conflict's fraction of total project decline or read a preference from non-use alone.

## AGR-K09

The IFPRI evaluation associates project access with production and income gains but reports substitution away from other crops and nutrition pathways involving illness, water and maternal work; lower irrigation yields can coexist with better household allocation.

**Cases:** AGR-C03. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [AGR-S06: Irrigation Technology and Commercialization of Rice in The Gambia: Effects on Income and Nutrition](https://ageconsearch.umn.edu/record/42167/files/rr75.pdf), printed pp. 9–13 and 106–108; survey-design passages. Supports: Production/income findings, crop substitution, nutritional conditions and good-rainfall-year allocation argument.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Irrigation changes production opportunities and labour allocation -> household income can rise while other crops shrink -> nutrition also depends on illness, sanitation and care time.
- **strongest rival:** Selection of better-resourced or differently located households into project access may partly explain observed income and nutrition differences.
- **discriminating observation:** Longitudinal comparable households or plausibly exogenous access changes, tracking all crops, person-level control and health, would better separate project effects from selection.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S06 summaries and conclusions were read through OCR; they report gains and substitution, explicitly discuss good-rainfall-year reallocation and caution about the observational design.
- **remaining ambiguity:** The review did not reproduce regressions or reconcile the same households and years with AGR-S05; total gains and control losses can both be true.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Carney's later field account documents conflicts and operating problems. Household totals can mask unequal control or burdens within a household.

**Inference limit:** Observational, largely cross-sectional evaluation; authors caution about latent variables and dependencies. Early gains are not a controlled rebuttal to later decline or distributional harm.

## AGR-K10

Pingali's retrospective describes modern crop varieties as part of an adaptation and input system, with uneven early benefits across crops and environments; shorter maturity, suitable water and local breeding could change the feasible cropping calendar.

**Cases:** AGR-C04. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [AGR-S07: Green Revolution: Impacts, limits, and the path ahead](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3411969/), First GR; Limitations of GR-Led Growth Strategies; Marginal Production Environments. Supports: International breeding, national adaptation, irrigation/input complements and ecological unevenness.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Locally suited varieties and supporting water/input conditions change yield and maturity -> extra or more productive crops become feasible -> adoption is more attractive in matching environments.
- **strongest rival:** Public investment, procurement, price policy or credit may drive both input access and adoption, making ecological suitability alone insufficient.
- **discriminating observation:** Compare the same released variety across plausibly independent changes in local adaptation, water access or prices, with maturity and realised output measured.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S07 distinguishes favourable and marginal production environments and describes shorter maturity and adaptation; no component-by-component causal experiment was undertaken in this contribution.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Research attention and infrastructure are themselves selected toward certain crops and regions; the review does not quantify each complement's independent contribution.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Different varieties and rainfed settings require different complements; cash, access and policy also matter, and irrigation is not a universal prerequisite for improved seed.

**Inference limit:** A synthesis of historical agricultural research, not an independent test of each component or a claim that all packages were privately or socially beneficial.

## AGR-K11

Gollin, Hansen and Wingender estimate a 44% direct yield gain in the average country from modern varieties during 1965–2010, holding allocations fixed, in their historical econometric framework; the baseline country sample contains 90 countries.

**Cases:** AGR-C04. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** causal-identification.

- [AGR-S08: Two Blades of Grass: The Impact of the Green Revolution](https://gwern.net/doc/economics/2021-gollin.pdf), pp.2346–2347,2358–2369 (average-country unit at p.2369),2378–2379. Supports: Release-timing design, sample, direct yield contribution and caveats on the no-Green-Revolution counterfactual.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Variety releases create crop-specific opportunities -> modern-variety adoption changes crop yields -> release timing and initial crop shares identify a predicted yield contribution under maintained assumptions.
- **strongest rival:** Crop-specific infrastructure, policies, demand or other research shocks correlated with release dates could generate similar relative-yield changes.
- **discriminating observation:** Pre-release relative trends, event-study patterns and coincident crop interventions test the design; effects preceding releases or vanishing after credible concurrent-shock controls would weaken attribution.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S08 design, event-study/robustness discussion, sample restrictions and counterfactual caveats were inspected. The reported direct gain is 44%, separate from additional allocation effects.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Inspection does not reproduce the dataset or prove parallel counterfactual trends. Authors expressly acknowledge that absent modern varieties other innovation might have occurred.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Crop-specific concurrent changes, spillovers, sample exclusions or different counterfactual innovation could change the estimate. Reallocation effects are additional rather than part of the stated direct gain.

**Inference limit:** An attributed estimate under relative-yield trend and exclusion assumptions, not a 44% gain for every farmer, a welfare share or an unqualified full-history counterfactual.

## AGR-K12

Rodell and colleagues estimate groundwater depletion of 17.7 ± 4.5 km³ per year in Rajasthan, Punjab and Haryana including Delhi during August 2002–October 2008 and judge irrigation and other human withdrawals the likely cause after examining other storage components.

**Cases:** AGR-C04. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [AGR-S09: Satellite-based estimates of groundwater depletion in India](https://fore.yale.edu/files/rodell_velicogna_famiglietti.pdf), pp. 999–1002 and Methods. Supports: GRACE-based regional estimate, hydrological subtraction, uncertainty and alternative water-storage components.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Human extraction exceeds replenishment -> regional groundwater stock declines -> satellite total-storage change remains after estimated non-groundwater components are removed.
- **strongest rival:** Changes in soil moisture, surface water, snow or other storage, or model error, could explain part of the gravity signal without comparable groundwater extraction.
- **discriminating observation:** A credible independent regional water balance and well-level evidence; explaining the observed signal through other storage components would weaken the groundwater attribution.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S09 was inspected for the regional boundary, observation window and investigation of precipitation and competing storage components, which support the extraction interpretation.
- **remaining ambiguity:** No independent reprocessing of satellite observations was done. The contribution deliberately does not infer which agricultural technology or policy caused what share of extraction.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Gravity observations measure total storage changes; component models, spatial resolution and unmeasured storage changes affect attribution and uncertainty.

**Inference limit:** A regional 2002–2008 depletion estimate, not a current rate, an all-India estimate or a seed-specific treatment effect. Modern varieties, electricity prices, pumping access and crop policies are not separately identified.

## AGR-K13

Goldstein and Udry link practical security of land control in Akwapim to longer fallow and subsequent agricultural returns, making the expectation of recovering an uncultivated plot a complement to investment through waiting.

**Cases:** AGR-C05. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [AGR-S10: The Profits of Power: Land Rights and Agricultural Investment in Ghana](https://www.parisschoolofeconomics.com/gurgand-marc/PolPubWeb/GoldsteinUdry2008.pdf), pp. 983–988 and sections IV.C–E. Supports: Land-control mechanism, fallow investment, political position and productivity analysis.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Risk of losing an uncultivated plot reduces the expected captured return to waiting -> insecure cultivators shorten fallow -> soil recovery and subsequent returns are reduced.
- **strongest rival:** Political position may proxy for labour, wealth or land characteristics that affect both fallow and output independently of expropriation risk.
- **discriminating observation:** Comparable plots with different security for the same cultivator, or credible security changes followed by altered fallow before output, distinguish the mechanism from fixed personal ability.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S10 provides within-household and plot-provenance contrasts, political-position analysis and soil/plot controls; these address several ability and land-quality alternatives.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Political-status exclusion restrictions remain contestable. Within-person differences do not remove every plot-specific or negotiated-access difference.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Connected cultivators may also differ in wealth, labour access or plot quality; fallowing is not the only possible way to maintain soil fertility.

**Inference limit:** Two-year observational survey with instrumental-variable arguments, not random tenure assignment. Exact survey calendar dates are not stated in the inspected description, so the case is dated late twentieth century.

## AGR-K14

Goldstein and Udry's within-household and within-cultivator contrasts support an institutional interpretation of the observed gender investment gap: women less often occupy powerful positions and face weaker practical security, rather than the difference directly revealing skill or taste.

**Cases:** AGR-C05. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [AGR-S10: The Profits of Power: Land Rights and Agricultural Investment in Ghana](https://www.parisschoolofeconomics.com/gurgand-marc/PolPubWeb/GoldsteinUdry2008.pdf), pp. 1002–1018; tables 7–9 and Conclusion. Supports: Gender, local power, plot routes, fallow and estimated return differences.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Unequal access to local authority changes security during fallow -> expected private returns differ by cultivator -> investment and productivity can differ without a skill gap.
- **strongest rival:** Unmeasured labour constraints or plot selection associated with gender could generate some of the same outcomes even if security were equal.
- **discriminating observation:** Gender differences shrinking on plots with comparable secure control for the same people, and following enforceable rights changes, would favour the security channel over a fixed ability account.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S10's tables 7–9 and conclusion compare land-acquisition routes and within-cultivator variation and explicitly connect gender differences to local political position.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Observed rights and power remain entangled with other resources; the study supports a pathway more strongly than the independent size of every component.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Wealth, access and gendered obligations may also differ; observed institutional disadvantage does not establish a single remedy or eliminate all unobserved constraints.

**Inference limit:** The evidence does not randomly assign political status or establish formal individual title as uniquely effective. Gender is not itself an independently isolated agronomic treatment.

## AGR-K15

In Karlan and colleagues' northern Ghana experiments, rainfall-index insurance increased agricultural expenditure and shifted production choices toward greater risk, while cash grants alone produced relatively small investment responses.

**Cases:** AGR-C06. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** causal-identification.

- [AGR-S11: Agricultural Decisions after Relaxing Credit and Risk Constraints](https://www.atai-research.org/wp-content/uploads/2016/07/Agricultural-Decisions-after-Relaxing-Credit-and-Risk-Constraints.pdf), pp. 599–601; experiment description and investment results, including tables III–IV. Supports: Randomised cash/insurance interventions and measured farm investment responses.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Insurance reduces a specified rainfall downside -> farmers can mobilise resources and accept greater production risk -> agricultural expenditure and choices respond.
- **strongest rival:** A general resource transfer or programme-attention effect, rather than downside-risk protection, could increase investment.
- **discriminating observation:** Randomly assigned insurance alone, cash alone, both and comparison conditions allow different responses to be compared; contracts of comparable value but different risk protection would further separate channels.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S11 reports insurance-only investment responses and relatively modest cash-only responses in the randomised design, which weakens a simple cash-shortfall-only explanation for the sampled decisions.
- **remaining ambiguity:** State-contingent income, learning and expectations coexist with risk reduction; treatment magnitudes differ and the experiment is not a universal constraint ranking.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** The response varies by treatment, year and context; insurance and cash are not identical transfers in timing, state contingency or magnitude.

**Inference limit:** Causal effect of the tested offers/contracts on this sample, not proof that cash constraints never matter or that greater investment raises profits or welfare.

## AGR-K16

Subsequent demand for the Ghana rainfall-index insurance varied with price and experienced or network-observed payouts; the paper considers trust, recency and basis risk and leaves the welfare interpretation of greater investment unresolved.

**Cases:** AGR-C06. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** contested.

- [AGR-S11: Agricultural Decisions after Relaxing Credit and Risk Constraints](https://www.atai-research.org/wp-content/uploads/2016/07/Agricultural-Decisions-after-Relaxing-Credit-and-Risk-Constraints.pdf), sections VI–VIII, pp. 637–650. Supports: Demand/pricing analyses, payout experience and networks, competing interpretations and profit/welfare caveats.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Contract price and observed payment experience affect perceived value or credibility -> insurance demand changes -> availability of the risk-management complement itself depends on adoption conditions.
- **strongest rival:** Concern about basis risk or rational learning about delivery could produce the same payout-sensitive demand attributed to distrust or recency.
- **discriminating observation:** Independently vary credible payment guarantees, payout–loss correlation and information while holding premium conditions comparable; distinguish experienced from randomly assigned informational exposure.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S11 price and payout/network analyses and the conclusion were inspected. Authors discuss several explanations and unresolved investment-profit/welfare links.
- **remaining ambiguity:** The observed responses do not uniquely identify beliefs or bias, and increased input use is not sufficient evidence of net welfare gains.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Experienced payouts may update rational beliefs about reliability or contract usefulness; their association with demand need not show a behavioural error. Limited profit precision does not prove zero benefit.

**Inference limit:** Random price variation is stronger evidence about price response than observational payout/network variation is about a unique psychological mechanism. Full-cost renewal and insurer sustainability are not established.

## AGR-K17

The Odisha improved-biomass-stove experiment found an initial reduction in primary cooks' carbon-monoxide exposure that disappeared by the following year; the inspected 2012 working paper follows 2,651 households in 44 villages for up to four years.

**Cases:** AGR-C07. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** causal-identification.

- [AGR-S12: Up in Smoke: The Influence of Household Behavior on the Long-Run Impact of Improved Cooking Stoves (2012 working paper)](https://www.hks.harvard.edu/sites/default/files/centers/cid/files/publications/faculty-working-papers/241_HannaDufloGreenstone.pdf), sections II and VI; introduction and conclusion. Supports: Phased random assignment, sample, follow-up and time path of exposure effects.
- [AGR-S16: Up in Smoke: The Influence of Household Behavior on the Long-Run Impact of Improved Cooking Stoves (published article)](https://www.povertyactionlab.org/sites/default/files/research-paper/52%20Up%20in%20Smoke%20AEJ2016.pdf), published abstract. Supports: Confirms the central initial-exposure-reduction and later-disappearance result in the final journal abstract.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Programme supplies an improved stove and training -> households initially change cooking -> exposure falls, but continued-use/maintenance conditions alter the effect over subsequent years.
- **strongest rival:** Seasonal exposure shifts or differential follow-up rather than programme performance could generate an apparent fading effect.
- **discriminating observation:** Compare treatment and control households at equivalent follow-up times and inspect continued use, maintenance and attrition; persistence despite maintained operation would challenge a use/repair explanation of fading.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S12 reports repeated outcomes under phased random assignment and measured usage/maintenance; AGR-S16 confirms the central time pattern only in its abstract.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Randomised offer identifies programme effects; the individual mediators are not each randomly assigned, so their separate causal shares are not identified.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Initial gains occurred, and sustained effects might differ for another design, fuel or service model; this is not a null result for every clean-cooking technology.

**Inference limit:** Exact sample and methods locators refer to the 2012 full working paper. The 2016 final full text was not acquired; its abstract is not a second experiment or independent replication.

## AGR-K18

The Odisha working paper documents continued use of older stoves, breakage and maintenance problems alongside the loss of exposure gains, no expected fuel savings or clear health improvements, and additional repair time.

**Cases:** AGR-C07. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [AGR-S12: Up in Smoke: The Influence of Household Behavior on the Long-Run Impact of Improved Cooking Stoves (2012 working paper)](https://www.hks.harvard.edu/sites/default/files/centers/cid/files/publications/faculty-working-papers/241_HannaDufloGreenstone.pdf), sections V–VII; discussion and conclusion, printed pp. 27–32. Supports: Usage, maintenance, fuel, time, health and interpretation of the real programme's performance.
- [AGR-S16: Up in Smoke: The Influence of Household Behavior on the Long-Run Impact of Improved Cooking Stoves (published article)](https://www.povertyactionlab.org/sites/default/files/research-paper/52%20Up%20in%20Smoke%20AEJ2016.pdf), published pp.86–87,106–108. Supports: Training and local promoters were present; repair/rebuilding campaign was not independently randomized.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Hypothesized mediation: stove condition, use, demand and recurring repair interact with exposure; the randomized offer identifies the package effect, not a separate maintenance effect.
- **strongest rival:** The stove's physical emissions reduction may be insufficient even when correctly used, or other pollution sources may dominate exposure; maintenance is then not the sole limiting channel.
- **discriminating observation:** Randomly vary reliable repair support or design while measuring actual combustion, stacking and personal exposure, rather than comparing owners alone.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S12 documents maintenance/usage patterns and repair time and links these to fading results; a separate randomisation of all mediators was not found.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Correlation among use, damage and preferences permits reverse and common-cause explanations. The trial estimates the package's effects more cleanly than each mechanism. Training/promoters were already part of the package. Adequacy cannot be defined after an unwanted outcome; design/task fit may affect both use and repair.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Meal needs, fuel, training, stove design, household choices and repair quality can all contribute; more effort or correct use cannot be assumed costless or guaranteed to restore benefit.

**Inference limit:** Observed mediators and randomised programme outcomes do not separately identify every behavioural mechanism. These costs belong to the delivered service, rather than proving uninformed users or universal resistance.

## AGR-K19

The HAPIN trial's package of free LPG stoves, continuing home-delivered fuel, instruction and adherence support achieved high use and markedly lower measured personal particulate exposure among pregnant women in selected sites in Guatemala, India, Peru and Rwanda.

**Cases:** AGR-C07. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** causal-identification.

- [AGR-S13: Liquefied Petroleum Gas or Biomass for Cooking and Effects on Birth Weight](https://stacks.cdc.gov/view/cdc/124935/cdc_124935_DS1.pdf), author-manuscript pp. 6–11; Methods, adherence and exposure Results. Supports: Randomisation, intervention support, measured adherence and personal exposure contrast.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** LPG equipment plus delivered fuel and support changes feasible everyday cooking -> high adherence displaces biomass use -> measured personal particulate exposure declines.
- **strongest rival:** General monitoring or other trial behaviour might affect exposure, while equipment alone might be sufficient in some settings; component contributions need not equal the full-package effect.
- **discriminating observation:** Randomised package versus control with exposure monitoring identifies its effect; factorial changes to fuel support and maintenance would identify which complements sustain use.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S13 describes randomisation, supply/adherence procedures and lower measured exposure with high adherence; no commercial-adoption test is claimed.
- **remaining ambiguity:** The individual contributions of device, free fuel, delivery and support remain bundled, and study monitoring may differ from ordinary delivery.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Residual exposure remained, and the outcome is under extensive free support rather than a market adoption environment.

**Inference limit:** No claim of unsubsidised willingness to pay, post-support persistence, superiority of LPG per unit cost or head-to-head comparability with Odisha. Participants and technologies differ.

## AGR-K20

Despite high adherence and lower exposure, HAPIN's adjusted birth-weight difference was 19.6 g with a 95% confidence interval of −10.1 to 49.2 g; this is a null finding for that specified outcome, not evidence of no health benefits of any kind.

**Cases:** AGR-C07. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** causal-identification.

- [AGR-S13: Liquefied Petroleum Gas or Biomass for Cooking and Effects on Birth Weight](https://stacks.cdc.gov/view/cdc/124935/cdc_124935_DS1.pdf), author-manuscript Results and Discussion, pp. 8–11. Supports: Adjusted birth-weight estimate, confidence interval and outcome-specific discussion.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** The assigned supported LPG package reduces exposure during the observed pregnancy interval -> any resulting fetal-growth effect is measured as an intention-to-treat birth-weight contrast.
- **strongest rival:** An exposure–birth-weight mechanism may exist but be too small at the achieved exposure difference or intervention timing; a lack of adherence is a weaker explanation here because adherence was high.
- **discriminating observation:** Earlier intervention or lower residual exposure with otherwise comparable delivery would distinguish timing/threshold explanations; sufficiently powered outcome measurements would constrain small effects.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S13's adjusted estimate and interval were inspected alongside high adherence/exposure reduction, directly limiting the explanation that all null outcomes arose from little use.
- **remaining ambiguity:** A confidence interval for an intervention effect does not select among residual exposure, timing and other biological explanations or prove a zero effect.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** A small benefit within the interval remains compatible with the data. Intervention timing, residual exposure and other fetal-growth determinants may limit changes in birth weight.

**Inference limit:** One primary outcome from one trial paper; other HAPIN outcomes were not evaluated here. The null does not identify which biological or implementation explanation is correct.

## AGR-K21

Cowan's history documents household machines that still required attendance and manual handling and a reorganisation of middle-class American domestic work; she treats some advertising and rising-standard explanations as hypotheses rather than measured causal effects.

**Cases:** AGR-C08. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [AGR-S14: The Industrial Revolution in the Home: Household Technology and Social Change in the 20th Century](https://paperzz.com/doc/9496641/-industrial-revolution--in-the-home), pp. 4–10, 13–15 and 21–23. Supports: Attended machine work, domestic service, childcare/consumption standards and explicit historical hypotheses.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Appliances change particular operations -> handling, standards and allocation of work can change concurrently -> reduced exertion need not translate one-for-one into fewer household hours.
- **strongest rival:** Demographic changes, domestic-service prices or market work may independently explain household time patterns, with appliances following rather than causing those changes.
- **discriminating observation:** Comparable households with independent appliance access changes, measured service quantity, purchased help, exertion and individual time would separate technical substitution from changing standards and selection.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S14 gives historical operating descriptions and discusses changing domestic-service and consumption arrangements; it explicitly qualifies some causal interpretations.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Qualitative sequences support plausible reorganisation but do not isolate national appliance effects or all households' preferences.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Less physical exertion can be valuable even when hours remain, and advertising content is not proof of what every household did or preferred.

**Inference limit:** Mainly middle-class documentary population; magazine archives were accessed through Cowan's analysis, not independently surveyed. Historical machinery examples are not national adoption rates.

## AGR-K22

In Ramey's inspected 2008 reconstruction, weekly home-production time for women aged 18–64 falls from about 46.8 hours in 1900 to 40.9 in 1965 and 29.3 in 2005; men's time rises and aggregate interpretations depend on composition and denominator.

**Cases:** AGR-C08. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [AGR-S15: Time Spent in Home Production in the 20th Century: New Estimates from Old Data](https://www.nber.org/system/files/working_papers/w13985/w13985.pdf), sections V–VIII and table 6. Supports: Reconstructed age/sex time series and aggregate accounting.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** This reports the reconstructed time series and its denominator; no causal share is assigned to appliances.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Early observations are heterogeneous and require harmonisation, grouped-data estimation and extrapolation; later surveys are more representative.

**Inference limit:** Numbers refer to the 2008 working-paper version, not unacquired final journal tables. This is a national reconstruction and not an appliance-specific causal estimate or measure of work strenuousness.

## AGR-K23

Cowan and Ramey together show why persistent housework hours alone cannot establish that appliances had no value, while declining hours alone cannot identify how much appliances caused: output, effort, purchased services and who performs the work can all change.

**Cases:** AGR-C08. **Evidence:** synthesis-inference. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** hypothesis.

- [AGR-S14: The Industrial Revolution in the Home: Household Technology and Social Change in the 20th Century](https://paperzz.com/doc/9496641/-industrial-revolution--in-the-home), pp. 21–23. Supports: Historical distinction between reorganisation of household work and simple labour elimination.
- [AGR-S15: Time Spent in Home Production in the 20th Century: New Estimates from Old Data](https://www.nber.org/system/files/working_papers/w13985/w13985.pdf), introduction and sections VII–VIII. Supports: Time-use definitions, composition and limits of interpreting long-run changes.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** proposed
- **mechanism chain:** Technology changes operation costs while households adjust output and task allocation -> measured hours combine technical, demand and distribution changes -> a time series alone does not isolate technical value.
- **strongest rival:** A fixed-output, fixed-household task with little adjustment may exhibit direct, near-one-for-one time savings, making the broader reorganisation explanation unnecessary locally.
- **discriminating observation:** Measure both task quantity/quality and inputs before and after independent appliance-access changes, including purchased help and effort; stable output with a direct fall in hours would support the simple substitution account for that task.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S14 documents work reorganisation; AGR-S15 reconstructs time trends and denominator issues. Neither inspected source provides the proposed appliance-access design.
- **remaining ambiguity:** A useful accounting warning is better supported than a precise claim about which mechanism dominates total historical change.
- **hypothesis timing:** retrospective synthesis

**Counterevidence/rival:** A particular appliance may directly save time for a particular task; the qualification does not imply time saving is absent or unmeasurable.

**Inference limit:** Methodological inference from differently scoped evidence, not a pooled estimate or independent appliance experiment. Historical source traditions overlap and complete genealogy is unknown.

## AGR-K24

Across these selected cases, testing a device or plot is often insufficient to evaluate the larger system that must operate, and gains in adoption, effort or output do not alone establish who benefits or whether welfare improves.

**Cases:** AGR-C01; AGR-C02; AGR-C03; AGR-C04; AGR-C05; AGR-C06; AGR-C07; AGR-C08. **Evidence:** synthesis-inference. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** hypothesis.

- [AGR-S02: Energetic efficiency and political expediency in Titicaca Basin raised field agriculture](https://www.academia.edu/6455173/Energetic_Efficiency_and_Political_Expediency_in_Titicaca_Basin_Raised_Field_Agriculture), pp. 280–288. Supports: Rotation/maintenance accounting challenges initial-plot comparisons.
- [AGR-S05: Contract Farming and Female Rice Growers in The Gambia](https://media.odi.org/documents/8169.pdf), pp. 8–18. Supports: Control of crop returns and labour participation complicate aggregate project performance.
- [AGR-S11: Agricultural Decisions after Relaxing Credit and Risk Constraints](https://www.atai-research.org/wp-content/uploads/2016/07/Agricultural-Decisions-after-Relaxing-Credit-and-Risk-Constraints.pdf), pp. 648–650. Supports: Explicit unresolved investment-profit/welfare interpretation.
- [AGR-S12: Up in Smoke: The Influence of Household Behavior on the Long-Run Impact of Improved Cooking Stoves (2012 working paper)](https://www.hks.harvard.edu/sites/default/files/centers/cid/files/publications/faculty-working-papers/241_HannaDufloGreenstone.pdf), sections V–VII. Supports: Continued operation and maintenance alter the delivered cooking effect.
- [AGR-S13: Liquefied Petroleum Gas or Biomass for Cooking and Effects on Birth Weight](https://stacks.cdc.gov/view/cdc/124935/cdc_124935_DS1.pdf), author-manuscript pp. 6–11. Supports: Supported uptake and exposure reduction coexist with outcome-specific null finding.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** proposed
- **mechanism chain:** A local technical change interacts with operation, complements and control -> private choices and sustained performance differ from an isolated demonstration -> distribution and welfare require additional outcome measures.
- **strongest rival:** Selection for well-known problematic cases may manufacture apparent generality; in many bounded tasks standalone technical performance may predict uptake and benefit adequately.
- **discriminating observation:** Prospectively sample both straightforward substitutions and complex programmes, state the actor/unit/outcome before observing results, and test whether added system variables improve predictions beyond device performance and price.
- **evidence sought found:** AGR-S02, AGR-S05, AGR-S11, AGR-S12 and AGR-S13 supply heterogeneous examples and explicit limits, not a representative historical sample or prospective model comparison.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Selection, differing outcomes and connected scholarship prevent prevalence or universal claims. The proposed discriminating study has not been performed here.
- **hypothesis timing:** retrospective synthesis

**Counterevidence/rival:** Cases were purposively selected for causal contrasts and complications; settings where a simple equipment substitution works well are not represented proportionately.

**Inference limit:** A research heuristic, not a universal adoption law, a ranked mechanism list or an estimate of mechanism frequency. Recurrence does not establish independent corroboration or transferable effect sizes.

## AGR-K25

Carney reports that continued Jahaly-Pacharr plot access depended on production-credit repayment; in summer 1990 management seized plots after arrears, then allowed conditional reclamation after repayment the next season.

**Cases:** AGR-C03. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [AGR-S05: Contract Farming and Female Rice Growers in The Gambia](https://media.odi.org/documents/8169.pdf), Network Paper15 printed pp.5–6, section3. Supports: Repayment condition, threat of usufruct loss, dated seizures and negotiated conditional return.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Records a dated reported sanction and negotiated response, not the separately identified effect of sanctions on labour supply.

**Counterevidence/rival:** The episode includes a compromise and differentiated outside options; it does not establish that every participant or every workday was coerced.

**Inference limit:** Attributed qualitative field account; not a measured share of output or labour caused by sanctions. Project enforcement and household control can interact.

## ANC-K01

Hero's transmitted description specifies steam-driven rotation of a sphere but no productive load or output measurement.

**Cases:** ANC-C01. **Evidence:** historical-primary. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [ANC-S01: The Pneumatics of Hero of Alexandria from the original Greek](https://www.gutenberg.org/files/77400/77400-h/77400-h.htm), §50, p. 72; preface pp. vii–viii. Supports: Mechanism and editorial framing of the inspected edition.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Bounded statement about what the inspected passage says and omits.

**Counterevidence/rival:** A text's omission does not establish absence of experiments elsewhere.

**Inference limit:** Description is not proof of ancient deployment, first invention, or prevalence.

## ANC-K02

Roby offers a philosophical reading of Hero's sphere as an alternative to judging it as an unsuccessful industrial engine; original purpose remains contested.

**Cases:** ANC-C01. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** contested.

- [ANC-S02: The Mechanical Tradition of Hero of Alexandria: Strategies of Reading from Antiquity to the Early Modern Period](https://assets.cambridge.org/97810090/14052/excerpt/9781009014052_excerpt.pdf), Introduction pp. 1–4. Supports: Critique of teleology and discussion of Keyser's philosophical interpretation.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** A demonstration aimed at analysing motion -> a text specifies visible rotation -> no productive load is needed for that purpose.
- **strongest rival:** A practical experimental device may omit applications or have had lost uses.
- **discriminating observation:** Ancient user testimony or a connected working apparatus associated with a specified purpose.
- **evidence sought found:** ANC-S01 passage and ANC-S02 introductory interpretation; no user account found.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Both purposes can predict the same surviving description.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Other Heronian devices have practical applications; the corpus permits several uses.

**Inference limit:** The original Keyser paper and full Roby monograph were not read.

## ANC-K03

The inspected Hero sources do not establish sustained productive operation or commercial cost competitiveness of the rotating sphere.

**Cases:** ANC-C01. **Evidence:** synthesis-inference. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [ANC-S01: The Pneumatics of Hero of Alexandria from the original Greek](https://www.gutenberg.org/files/77400/77400-h/77400-h.htm), §50, p. 72. Supports: No load, duration or cost record.
- [ANC-S02: The Mechanical Tradition of Hero of Alexandria: Strategies of Reading from Antiquity to the Early Modern Period](https://assets.cambridge.org/97810090/14052/excerpt/9781009014052_excerpt.pdf), pp. 1–4. Supports: Warns against reading modern industrial goals into the device.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Evidence-scope statement; no causal claim explaining ancient non-adoption.

**Counterevidence/rival:** A reconstruction could show useful work, but would not itself establish ancient commercial adoption.

**Inference limit:** This is a boundary of this source set, not a claim that such operation was physically impossible.

## ANC-K04

Barbegal's sixteen-wheel installation and carbonate traces establish productive hydraulic machinery and repeated operation at a particular Roman site.

**Cases:** ANC-C02. **Evidence:** archaeological. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [ANC-S03: The second century CE Roman watermills of Barbegal: Unraveling the enigma of one of the oldest industrial complexes](https://doi.org/10.1126/sciadv.aar3620), Introduction; Results: microstratigraphy; Figs. 1, 3–4. Supports: Site configuration and operational deposits.
- [ANC-S04: Operation and decline of the Barbegal mill complex, the largest industrial complex of antiquity](https://doi.org/10.1002/gea.22016), §2 Study site; §6 Conclusions. Supports: Configuration and terminal operation of selected structures.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Site-level deployment evidence, not a diffusion or causal estimate.

**Counterevidence/rival:** The sixteen wooden wheels do not survive; reconstruction combines architecture and casts.

**Inference limit:** No empire-wide adoption share, exact annual output, or purchaser identity follows.

## ANC-K05

Barbegal's repeated seasonal interruptions support a variable operating schedule; ship's biscuit production is a plausible but unconfirmed market explanation.

**Cases:** ANC-C02. **Evidence:** archaeological. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** contested.

- [ANC-S03: The second century CE Roman watermills of Barbegal: Unraveling the enigma of one of the oldest industrial complexes](https://doi.org/10.1126/sciadv.aar3620), Results: Stable isotope composition, Fig. 4; Discussion. Supports: Truncated seasonal sequences and the authors' hardtack interpretation.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Seasonal shipping demand -> mill managers schedule production -> recurrent late-summer/autumn pauses.
- **strongest rival:** Seasonal water limits or a different flour market could cause the same schedule.
- **discriminating observation:** Independent seasonal water reconstruction plus securely associated baking, storage, shipment or purchasing records.
- **evidence sought found:** ANC-S03 isotope results and alternative-market discussion inspected; no direct customer record.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Production purpose is inferred from timing and location; alternatives not eliminated.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Water availability, other seasonal markets, or other operating choices might also produce pauses.

**Inference limit:** The carbonate records do not identify the product's final consumer.

## ANC-K06

Three western Barbegal flumes indicate staged terminal operation while aqueduct water continued; loss of water supply alone does not explain that observed sequence.

**Cases:** ANC-C02. **Evidence:** archaeological. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [ANC-S04: Operation and decline of the Barbegal mill complex, the largest industrial complex of antiquity](https://doi.org/10.1002/gea.22016), §5 discussion of flumes A–C, Fig. 9; §6 Conclusions, pp. 604–607. Supports: Different terminal sequences and continued water flow.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Equipment deterioration or changing use -> uneven withdrawal/alteration of machinery -> individual mills cease while water remains.
- **strongest rival:** Declining discharge could impair mills unevenly even without a complete cutoff.
- **discriminating observation:** Synchronous flow-rate and component histories across both trains, independently dated demand and repair evidence.
- **evidence sought found:** ANC-S04 flume microstratigraphy and conclusions; supply did not simply cease everywhere first.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Demand, materials, water quantity and organisational capacity remain entangled.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Continuing flow need not mean unchanged discharge or a fully adequate supply.

**Inference limit:** Neither the entire site's chronology nor a unique cause of abandonment is identified.

## ANC-K07

Roman productive waterpower and enslaved hydraulic maintenance contradict an absolute incompatibility between slavery and complex machinery; the effect of labour costs on marginal adoption remains unidentified.

**Cases:** ANC-C02; ANC-C03. **Evidence:** synthesis-inference. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** contested.

- [ANC-S03: The second century CE Roman watermills of Barbegal: Unraveling the enigma of one of the oldest industrial complexes](https://doi.org/10.1126/sciadv.aar3620), Introduction and Results. Supports: Productive watermill installation in the Roman period.
- [ANC-S05: The Aqueducts of Rome (De aquaeductu urbis Romae), Book II](https://penelope.uchicago.edu/Thayer/E/Roman/Texts/Frontinus/De_Aquis/Bennett/2%2A.html), §§116–118. Supports: Enslaved workers in a sophisticated hydraulic service.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** An absolute institutional prohibition predicts no productive machinery within the slaveholding Roman setting -> Barbegal is a counterexample.
- **strongest rival:** Slavery reduces adoption incentives for some tasks without prohibiting all machinery.
- **discriminating observation:** Task-matched adoption and ownership/cost evidence could test the conditional claim; no such series is available here.
- **evidence sought found:** ANC-S03 operating machinery and ANC-S05 enslaved maintenance arrangements found.
- **remaining ambiguity:** The universal version fails; the conditional economic proposition remains open.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Neither source identifies Barbegal workers' legal status or a comparison of machine and labour prices.

**Inference limit:** Rejects an absolute proposition only; it does not estimate a slavery effect or deny coercion.

## ANC-K08

Frontinus reports about 240 publicly owned and 460 imperial enslaved aqueduct workers organised into specialised maintenance functions.

**Cases:** ANC-C03. **Evidence:** historical-primary. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [ANC-S05: The Aqueducts of Rome (De aquaeductu urbis Romae), Book II](https://penelope.uchicago.edu/Thayer/E/Roman/Texts/Frontinus/De_Aquis/Bennett/2%2A.html), §§116–117. Supports: Reported headcounts, ownership and functions.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Attribution to a specific ancient account, with performance limits explicit.

**Counterevidence/rival:** His account also complains about diversion of workers to private tasks.

**Inference limit:** High confidence in the reported arrangement, not an independently verified staffing census.

## ANC-K09

Frontinus describes recurrent funding and timed, sequential shutdowns as arrangements for maintaining Rome's aqueduct service.

**Cases:** ANC-C03. **Evidence:** historical-primary. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [ANC-S05: The Aqueducts of Rome (De aquaeductu urbis Romae), Book II](https://penelope.uchicago.edu/Thayer/E/Roman/Texts/Frontinus/De_Aquis/Bennett/2%2A.html), §118; §§119–124. Supports: Treasury/imperial funding, advance preparation and shutdown procedures.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Funding and advance scheduling -> personnel/materials available during limited shutdowns -> intended preservation of supply.
- **strongest rival:** Actual reliability may have depended more on redundant routes and favourable water sources, with rules poorly observed.
- **discriminating observation:** Work records or dated repairs showing compliance and service changes before/after procedural changes.
- **evidence sought found:** ANC-S05 prescriptive and financial passages inspected; no independent operational audit found.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Administrative intention cannot be equated with realised performance.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Prescriptions may respond to poor compliance; funds and personnel alone do not guarantee repair quality.

**Inference limit:** No causal estimate of improved reliability or fiscal adequacy.

## ANC-K10

Bannon's evidence distinguishes civic aqueduct grants from other water rights and shows roles for local control, payment and status; infrastructure did not imply a single empire-wide administration or equal access.

**Cases:** ANC-C03. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [ANC-S06: Fresh Water in Roman Law: Rights and Policy](https://doi.org/10.1017/S007543581700079X), §III Civic Aqueducts; §V Conclusion. Supports: Distinct rights configurations and social/political dimensions.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Describes institutional distinctions in the inspected legal-historical analysis.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Open-access fountains also existed; elite grants do not imply universal exclusion.

**Inference limit:** Rules and examples cannot quantify household supply or the distribution of actual consumption.

## ANC-K11

Procopius reports aqueduct disruption stopping Rome's mills in 537, fodder scarcity limiting animal substitution, and Tiber boatmills restoring grinding while wells still supplied drinking water.

**Cases:** ANC-C03. **Evidence:** historical-primary. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [ANC-S07: Wars, Book V (Gothic War I), chapter 19](https://penelope.uchicago.edu/Thayer/E/Roman/Texts/Procopius/Wars/5C%2A.html), Wars V.19.13, 19–28. Supports: Reported sequence, constraint and alternative services.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Siege cuts aqueduct flow and limits fodder -> existing mills and animal alternatives are constrained -> river-driven boatmills substitute for grinding.
- **strongest rival:** Some milling may have continued elsewhere; the emergency innovation may be less comprehensive than the narrative claims.
- **discriminating observation:** Contemporary grain/mill records and securely dated traces of competing installations during the siege.
- **evidence sought found:** ANC-S07 explicitly links disruption, feed scarcity and boatmills; drinking water has other sources.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Detailed sequence is one literary witness and cannot quantify city-wide adequacy.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Narrative may magnify the general's resourcefulness and adequacy of replacement capacity.

**Inference limit:** No throughput, household coverage or proof of permanent aqueduct abandonment.

## ANC-K12

Excavated early Arabian galleries complicate a single Iranian origin narrative; Boucharlat's polycentric account remains a hypothesis under uneven dating and disputed water-source identifications.

**Cases:** ANC-C04. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** contested.

- [ANC-S08: Qanāt and Falaj: Polycentric and Multi-Period Innovations: Iran and the United Arab Emirates as Case Studies](https://www.academia.edu/124000172/Boucharlat_R_2017_Ancient_qan%C4%81t_and_falaj_polycentric_and_multi_period_innovations_Iran_and_the_United_Arab_Emirates_as_case_studies_in_A_N_Angelakis_E_Chiotis_S_Eslamian_H_Weingartner_eds_Underground_Aqueducts_Handbook_CRC_Press_Boca_Raton_FL_279_301), §§17.1–17.2.1; §§17.3.4–17.4.2. Supports: Regional evidence, disputed classifications and chronological limits.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Different local groundwater conditions -> locally adjusted gallery construction -> similar broad forms without necessary common origin.
- **strongest rival:** Diffusion followed by adaptation could generate the same variation.
- **discriminating observation:** Secure construction dates and distinctive shared techniques across plausible intermediate sites.
- **evidence sought found:** ANC-S08 regional evidence inspected; no complete transmission chain established.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Non-observation in Iran is especially weak where survey and dating are incomplete.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Poor excavation and dating in Iran can produce an apparent geographical priority.

**Inference limit:** Earliest known evidence is not first invention; resemblance is not transmission.

## ANC-K13

The al-Madam sequence of deepening and later silting is consistent with declining water availability followed by discontinued maintenance, but does not isolate environmental change from settlement reorganisation.

**Cases:** ANC-C04. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [ANC-S08: Qanāt and Falaj: Polycentric and Multi-Period Innovations: Iran and the United Arab Emirates as Case Studies](https://www.academia.edu/124000172/Boucharlat_R_2017_Ancient_qan%C4%81t_and_falaj_polycentric_and_multi_period_innovations_Iran_and_the_United_Arab_Emirates_as_case_studies_in_A_N_Angelakis_E_Chiotis_S_Eslamian_H_Weingartner_eds_Underground_Aqueducts_Handbook_CRC_Press_Boca_Raton_FL_279_301), §17.2.2, p. 287. Supports: Deepening, diminished flow interpretation and abandonment.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Lower water availability -> deepen gallery -> diminishing returns or disrupted upkeep -> abandonment.
- **strongest rival:** Political or economic relocation reduces maintenance first; silting follows.
- **discriminating observation:** Independent water-table indicators temporally preceding deepening, versus evidence of prior settlement/maintenance withdrawal.
- **evidence sought found:** ANC-S08 alteration and sediment sequence found; no independent local time series inspected.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Water and maintenance can change reciprocally; climatic attribution is underdetermined.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Reduced cleaning or changed settlement could itself increase silting and apparent hydraulic failure.

**Inference limit:** Underlying excavation sequence is mediated through a synthesis; regional climatic causation is not identified.

## ANC-K14

Polybius reports that earlier Persian rulers offered five generations of cultivation rights for bringing water to unirrigated land; the inspected translation does not explicitly state a tax exemption.

**Cases:** ANC-C04. **Evidence:** historical-primary. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [ANC-S09: Histories, Book X, chapter 28](https://penelope.uchicago.edu/Thayer/E/Roman/Texts/Polybius/10%2A.html), X.28.2–4. Supports: Reported incentive and expense in Paton's wording.
- [ANC-S08: Qanāt and Falaj: Polycentric and Multi-Period Innovations: Iran and the United Arab Emirates as Case Studies](https://www.academia.edu/124000172/Boucharlat_R_2017_Ancient_qan%C4%81t_and_falaj_polycentric_and_multi_period_innovations_Iran_and_the_United_Arab_Emirates_as_case_studies_in_A_N_Angelakis_E_Chiotis_S_Eslamian_H_Weingartner_eds_Underground_Aqueducts_Handbook_CRC_Press_Boca_Raton_FL_279_301), §17.4.2, p. 295. Supports: Critical contextualisation of the reported galleries.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Exact attribution to a transmitted report and its inspected translation; investment causation is separately hypothetical.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Other translations or legal interpretations may construe the benefit differently.

**Inference limit:** Local retrospective testimony is not an actual grant deed or proof of universal enforcement.

## ANC-K15

A gravity-fed groundwater gallery can supply its outlet only when water is available at a compatible elevation and the channel remains passable; knowing the technique alone does not establish local feasibility.

**Cases:** ANC-C04. **Evidence:** synthesis-inference. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [ANC-S08: Qanāt and Falaj: Polycentric and Multi-Period Innovations: Iran and the United Arab Emirates as Case Studies](https://www.academia.edu/124000172/Boucharlat_R_2017_Ancient_qan%C4%81t_and_falaj_polycentric_and_multi_period_innovations_Iran_and_the_United_Arab_Emirates_as_case_studies_in_A_N_Angelakis_E_Chiotis_S_Eslamian_H_Weingartner_eds_Underground_Aqueducts_Handbook_CRC_Press_Boca_Raton_FL_279_301), §17.1 and Figs. 17.1–17.2; §17.2.2. Supports: Geometry of gallery supply and locally adjusted depth.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Accessible groundwater and gradient -> gravity flow through a passable gallery -> outlet supply.
- **strongest rival:** An apparently similar tunnel may instead convey an external surface source.
- **discriminating observation:** Trace the collecting section, outlet elevations and water source; distinguish groundwater interception from conveyance.
- **evidence sought found:** ANC-S08 explicitly separates gallery types and water sources.
- **remaining ambiguity:** The physical relationship is clear; source classification of particular early galleries remains disputed.
- **hypothesis timing:** retrospective synthesis

**Counterevidence/rival:** Pumped or surface-fed supply can replace the service, but constitutes a different configuration.

**Inference limit:** Physical constraint, not a historical cost model or estimate of aquifer sustainability.

## ANC-K16

Peng's presentation of inscriptions dated 168 and 199 CE supports official involvement in Dujiangyan's late Han maintenance; his interpretation relates the work to organised labour obligations.

**Cases:** ANC-C05. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [ANC-S10: Dujiangyan's Annual Repair System in Ancient Times: From Jianpengyan Inscription by Shu Defender Li Bing in Qin Dynasty (古代都江堰岁修制度——从《秦蜀守李冰湔堋堰官碑》说起)](https://www.sohu.com/a/255708893_523159), §1 Early repair in archaeological materials; §4 Labour organisation. Supports: Inscription readings and labour interpretation.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Reports bounded attestations and an attributed interpretation, without estimating their effect on longevity.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Commemorative inscriptions may selectively praise officials; labour status is partly inferred.

**Inference limit:** Reproduction inspected, not original rubbings; no household burden or consent measure.

## ANC-K17

Extending Dujiangyan's regular repair system back to its Qin foundation is an inference; the date of the six-character maintenance rule remains disputed in the inspected scholarship.

**Cases:** ANC-C05. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** contested.

- [ANC-S10: Dujiangyan's Annual Repair System in Ancient Times: From Jianpengyan Inscription by Shu Defender Li Bing in Qin Dynasty (古代都江堰岁修制度——从《秦蜀守李冰湔堋堰官碑》说起)](https://www.sohu.com/a/255708893_523159), §1 opening discussion; §2 competing dates of the six-character rule. Supports: Explicit evidentiary reservation and competing chronological attributions.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Sediment-prone headworks -> repeated early repairs -> institutionalised routine by the foundation period.
- **strongest rival:** Formalisation occurred later, with retrospective attribution to Li Bing.
- **discriminating observation:** Contemporary Qin repair instructions or securely dated work episodes rather than later retrospective attribution.
- **evidence sought found:** ANC-S10 acknowledges missing decisive early evidence and reports disputed dates.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Functional plausibility cannot determine institutional chronology.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** The engineering need for periodic work and Qin waterwork legislation make early maintenance plausible.

**Inference limit:** Need for repairs does not establish an identical institution or uninterrupted service.

## ANC-K18

Zheng et al.'s model links Dujiangyan's geometry and flow regimes to sediment diversion and identifies dredging needs, but cannot demonstrate unchanged ancient geometry or uninterrupted operation.

**Cases:** ANC-C05. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [ANC-S11: Sustainability of the Dujiangyan Irrigation System for over 2000 Years—A Numerical Investigation of the Water and Sediment Dynamic Diversions](https://doi.org/10.3390/su12062431), §§1, 3–6; pp. 12–13 discussion and validation caveat. Supports: Modelled mechanisms, maintenance requirement, modern alterations and limits.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Specified geometry and inflow -> flow and sediment partition -> irrigation intake protected, with residual sediment requiring dredging.
- **strongest rival:** Different historic geometry, unmodelled regulation or alternative sediment parameters may produce different outcomes.
- **discriminating observation:** Validate against measured flows and sediment, then test independently reconstructed historic configurations and repair records.
- **evidence sought found:** ANC-S11 model, modern-dam exclusion and validation caveat inspected.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Physical plausibility in model conditions does not identify historical persistence.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research
