The Martu evidence supports a feedback hypothesis in which immediate small-game foraging benefits help maintain repeated burns and landscape heterogeneity without requiring a central planner to direct every ignition.

**Cases:** OCE-C03. **Evidence:** synthesis-inference. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [OCE-S07: The “fire stick farming” hypothesis: Australian Aboriginal foraging strategies, biodiversity, and anthropogenic fire mosaics](https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.0804757105), pp.14798–14800, Results and Discussion. Supports: Task-specific burning benefits and localized landscape response.
- [OCE-S08: Aboriginal hunting buffers climate-driven fire-size variability in Australia’s spinifex grasslands](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3387077/), Materials and Methods, Martu burning behavior. Supports: Gendered task differences, mobility and resulting fire patterns.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Task-level returns and skilled decisions -> repeated spatially patterned ignitions -> locally maintained mosaic -> further foraging benefits
- **strongest rival:** Burning is maintained principally by norms or intentional collective stewardship; returns correlate with practices but are not decisive
- **discriminating observation:** Task-specific adoption variation, expressed decisions and changes in burning when immediate returns change
- **evidence sought found:** S07 finds burning concentrated in small-game hunting and no comparable immediate return gain for large-game burning; S08 maps the spatial association
- **remaining ambiguity:** No experiment on motivations or norms; compatible mechanisms remain
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Kin obligations, cultural authority and longer-term ecological aims can coexist with immediate benefits; no absence of institutions is claimed.

**Inference limit:** Does not establish selfish optimization, universal stewardship, or sufficient incentives under new conditions.

## OCE-K13

Kuk’s published sequence differentiates mounded cultivation around 6,950–6,440 cal BP from ditch cultivation beginning around 4,350–3,980 cal BP; a later account maps multiple, locally varying Phase 3 ditch configurations.

**Cases:** OCE-C04. **Evidence:** archaeological. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [OCE-S09: Origins of Agriculture at Kuk Swamp in the Highlands of New Guinea](https://doi.org/10.1126/science.1085255), pp.189–191, Table 1 and archaeological evidence. Supports: Different phase dates and feature forms.
- [OCE-S10: Phase 3: The Emergence of Ditches](https://press-files.anu.edu.au/downloads/press/n2540/pdf/ch13.pdf), pp.221–225 and 236. Supports: Phase 3 networks, variable spatial use and earlier possible linear experiments.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Describes a local chronological and material distinction.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Dating boundaries are approximate; only excavated or exposed traces survive.

**Inference limit:** Not a regional diffusion curve, proof of centralized command or a global independent-invention verdict.

## OCE-K14

Kuk Phase 3 ditches plausibly combined drainage with social functions such as plot marking, while wetland use coexisted with nearby dryland cultivation; the sources cannot separate hydrological, tenure and risk-management drivers.

**Cases:** OCE-C04. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** contested.

- [OCE-S10: Phase 3: The Emergence of Ditches](https://press-files.anu.edu.au/downloads/press/n2540/pdf/ch13.pdf), pp.234–237. Supports: Wetland/dryland comparison and explicitly alternative social/ecological explanations.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Water and/or competing plot claims -> channel digging and maintenance -> drainable and demarcated cultivation spaces
- **strongest rival:** Primarily hydrological adaptation versus primarily territorial demarcation; both predict channels
- **discriminating observation:** Ditch layout versus hydraulic gradient and field boundaries, repair histories, independent tenure/settlement evidence
- **evidence sought found:** S10 reports hierarchical and nonhierarchical networks and reviews several motivations
- **remaining ambiguity:** Ancient property institutions and labor control remain unknown; functional coexistence does not distinguish primary cause
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Archaeology records channels more readily than rules, rights, intentions or dryland maintenance.

**Inference limit:** Contemporary ethnographic analogies do not establish ancient owners, coercion or division of labor.

## OCE-K15

The Phase 3–4 sequence at Kuk contains a several-hundred-year gap in wetland cultivation, while the reason remains unresolved among hydrological change, dryland alternatives and social disruption; it does not demonstrate disappearance of cultivation knowledge.

**Cases:** OCE-C04. **Evidence:** archaeological. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** contested.

- [OCE-S10: Phase 3: The Emergence of Ditches](https://press-files.anu.edu.au/downloads/press/n2540/pdf/ch13.pdf), p.237. Supports: Later Phase 3 around 2700–2500 years ago, Phase 4 around 2000 years ago; competing abandonment explanations.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Changed water, alternatives or social access -> wetland maintenance ends -> detectable gap before later ditch configuration
- **strongest rival:** Loss of know-how versus deliberate reallocation or exclusion from workable land
- **discriminating observation:** Synchronous local water rise, changes in dryland crops/settlement, or evidence of population displacement preceding cessation
- **evidence sought found:** S10 explicitly identifies the alternatives and says the precise reason is unknown
- **remaining ambiguity:** Available chronology does not select a cause; knowledge persistence leaves limited material traces
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Occupation or gardening beyond the sampled wetland could continue; other valleys have insufficient resolution for simultaneous cessation.

**Inference limit:** Local discontinuity is not a culture-wide technological loss or regional failure.

## OCE-K16

At M24/11 in northern Te Waipounamu, the 2024 study associates probable kūmara starch and other proposed crop remains with fourteenth-century cultivation contexts and identifies later kūmara remains after 1650.

**Cases:** OCE-C05. **Evidence:** archaeological. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [OCE-S13: American sweet potato and Asia-Pacific crop experimentation during early colonisation of temperate-climate Aotearoa/New Zealand](https://doi.org/10.15184/aqy.2024.143), Results, Table 1/Fig.10 and Discussion, pp.1383–1391. Supports: Context-specific microbotany, short-life dates and later deposits.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Reports bounded site evidence and authors’ probabilistic taxonomic interpretations.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Taxonomic identification relies on morphology and context; crop-abundance inference is not directly proportional to surviving starch counts.

**Inference limit:** No continent-wide arrival date, harvest quantity, uninterrupted cultivation or universal disappearance of other crops.

## OCE-K17

The Cook Strait experimental gardens demonstrate feasible growth of selected Māori kūmara cultivars under 1999–2002 conditions, but modern plant treatment, fencing, windbreaks, unreplicated plots and nontraditional storage prevent treating their yields as ancient farm performance.

**Cases:** OCE-C05. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [OCE-S11: Experimental Growing of Pre-European Cultivars of Kūmara (Sweet Potato, Ipomoea batatas [L.] Lam.) at the Southern Margins of Māori Horticulture](https://nzarchaeology.org/download/experimental-growing-of-pre-european-cultivars-of-kumara-sweet-potato-ipomoea-batatas-l-lam-at-the-southern-margins-of-maori-horticulture), pp.162,168–173,177–185. Supports: Trial design, returned/tissue-cultured collection, results, storage caveat and limits.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Separates an observed feasibility demonstration from historical adoption and causal comparison.

**Counterevidence/rival:** The experiments were explicitly exploratory and iwi authorities were consulted; these limits do not make practical growth results worthless.

**Inference limit:** Per-hectare extrapolation from tiny plots is not measured ancient output, adoption or labor productivity.

## OCE-K18

At Pōhatu, the 2025 collaborative study used Mana Whenua knowledge to guide questions and interpret modified soils; weak support for initially considered marine fertilizers led to further testing of terrestrial plant inputs, with kānuka a plausible rather than certain source.

**Cases:** OCE-C05. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [OCE-S12: Research at the interface between Indigenous knowledge and soil science; weaving knowledges to understand horticultural land use in Aotearoa / New Zealand](https://soil.copernicus.org/articles/11/583/2025/), sections 1.1–3,4.1–4.3,6; Fig.9. Supports: Research sequence, knowledge-guided interpretation, gravel additions and fertilizer alternatives.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Place-linked knowledge and shared questions -> targeted soil sampling and interpretation -> candidate amendments revised through joint discussion -> bounded reconstruction
- **strongest rival:** Soil chemistry alone would yield the same conclusions, or any favored tradition could be fitted after the fact
- **discriminating observation:** Documented sequence in which an initial candidate fails, further candidates are proposed, and tests discriminate rather than merely affirm
- **evidence sought found:** S12 section4.3 describes marine hypotheses failing Mn matching and expanded plant testing; sections1–3 document prior collaboration
- **remaining ambiguity:** No parallel research team without community input; exact ancient fertilizer and timing remain uncertain
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Geochemical signatures are not unique; only two profiles and selected candidate amendments were examined.

**Inference limit:** Knowledge systems contribute different evidence and meanings; agreement is not blanket authentication of all historical details.

## OCE-K19

Pōhatu’s contemporary investigation is framed by Mana Whenua as recovery of place-based cultivation knowledge after land alienation and disconnection; the published study supports reconnection and food-sovereignty aspirations but does not measure restored horticultural adoption.

**Cases:** OCE-C05. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [OCE-S12: Research at the interface between Indigenous knowledge and soil science; weaving knowledges to understand horticultural land use in Aotearoa / New Zealand](https://soil.copernicus.org/articles/11/583/2025/), Introduction and section1.1; Conclusion. Supports: Private ownership over 150 years, displacement narrative, research questions and intended restoration.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Distinguishes documented research purpose and historical account from unmeasured deployment outcomes.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Historical land-transfer details rely partly on cited scholarship and community history, not a deed audit in this contribution.

**Inference limit:** Do not convert an aspiration or research partnership into a completed livelihood transition.

## OCE-K20

The Martu study describes customary rights to burn, possible retribution for burning others’ areas, and social value attached to care for Country; localized burning was not institution-free individual optimization.

**Cases:** OCE-C03. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [OCE-S08: Aboriginal hunting buffers climate-driven fire-size variability in Australia’s spinifex grasslands](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3387077/), Materials and Methods, paragraphs on social/economic disincentives and kanyinin/Jukurrpa. Supports: Ethnographic account of rights, costs, responsibilities and public-good recognition.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Records the published ethnographic account of governance and meanings without assigning isolated causal weights.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Norms and sanctions are described by researchers, not quantified as an independent enforcement dataset.

**Inference limit:** No claim that all Martu follow identical rules, that enforcement is equal, or that social incentives alone determine fire outcomes.

## POL-K01

Enomoto reports 436 hunting firearms among 955 Shiiba-mountain households in 1745 and 586 firearms in 1836; these are local recorded stocks, not a national ownership rate.

**Cases:** POL-C01. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [POL-S01: Giving Up the Gun? Overcoming Myths about Japanese Sword-Hunting and Firearms Control](https://www.isc.meiji.ac.jp/~transfer/papers/en/pdf/06/04_Enomoto.pdf), p. 51, note 66. Supports: Counts inherited from Fujiki, Katanagari, pp. 168–169.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Bounded description of a published local example.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Household denominator for 1836 is not supplied; recorded ownership may exclude unlicensed guns.

**Inference limit:** Original village registers not inspected; neither persons armed nor annual use can be calculated.

## POL-K02

Legal crop-protection and hunting use, together with recorded commoner ownership, contradicts a general account of Tokugawa Japan eliminating guns.

**Cases:** POL-C01. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [POL-S01: Giving Up the Gun? Overcoming Myths about Japanese Sword-Hunting and Firearms Control](https://www.isc.meiji.ac.jp/~transfer/papers/en/pdf/06/04_Enomoto.pdf), II, pp. 50–53. Supports: Local firearm records and distinctions among possession, carriage and use.
- [POL-S02: The Social Life of Firearms in Tokugawa Japan](https://www.tandfonline.com/doi/abs/10.1080/10371390902780530?journalCode=cjst20), Abstract. Supports: Common rural firearms and farm-tool uses.
- [POL-S03: Introduction: the reception of the harquebus in East and South-East Asia](https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/international-journal-of-asian-studies/article/introduction-the-reception-of-the-harquebus-in-east-and-southeast-asia/AD08FBBF2D32EDBC28B3AE2D7200CBCF), Japan section, p. 9. Supports: Continued elite and non-elite peacetime niches.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Tests a categorical absence claim using positive local evidence; no causal estimate of regulation.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Restrictions and confiscations occurred; persistence does not imply unchanged military effectiveness or unconstrained ownership.

**Inference limit:** Sources share Japanese historical scholarship; high confidence applies to rejecting total elimination, not to national abundance.

## POL-K03

Task-specific permission and peacetime elite appropriation helped accommodate continued firearm use within Tokugawa hierarchies, rather than requiring universal rejection of the technology.

**Cases:** POL-C01. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [POL-S01: Giving Up the Gun? Overcoming Myths about Japanese Sword-Hunting and Firearms Control](https://www.isc.meiji.ac.jp/~transfer/papers/en/pdf/06/04_Enomoto.pdf), II–III, pp. 50–55. Supports: Differentiated regulation and village use.
- [POL-S03: Introduction: the reception of the harquebus in East and South-East Asia](https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/international-journal-of-asian-studies/article/introduction-the-reception-of-the-harquebus-in-east-and-southeast-asia/AD08FBBF2D32EDBC28B3AE2D7200CBCF), Japan section, p. 9. Supports: Licensed commoner use and elite marksmanship/status.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Crop/hunting needs and elite interests -> authorities authorize differentiated uses -> firearms retain legitimate niches -> persistence under regulation.
- **strongest rival:** Persistence merely reflects inability to enforce a universal ban.
- **discriminating observation:** Officially acknowledged permissible uses and elite use, not only illicit caches.
- **evidence sought found:** S01 records authorized local possession; S03 summarizes regulated farming and elite marksmanship.
- **remaining ambiguity:** No matched-domain enforcement comparison; peace, status and livelihood incentives co-evolve.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Enforcement weakness or unofficial circulation could also sustain guns; no estimate of their relative weight.

**Inference limit:** This is a scoped accommodation mechanism, not proof that peace or hierarchy caused a national innovation slowdown.

## POL-K04

Howell interprets a shift in Edo-hinterland gun meanings from farm implements toward weapons in the 1840s as responding to anxiety about rural disorder.

**Cases:** POL-C01. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** low. **Causal status:** hypothesis.

- [POL-S02: The Social Life of Firearms in Tokugawa Japan](https://www.tandfonline.com/doi/abs/10.1080/10371390902780530?journalCode=cjst20), Publisher abstract. Supports: Author states the 1840s change and disorder interpretation.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Perceived disorder -> villagers reinterpret guns as defensive weapons -> social use/meaning changes.
- **strongest rival:** A change in record language or administrative classification rather than in actual behavior.
- **discriminating observation:** Contemporaneous petitions and firing/use records showing changes before external military rearmament.
- **evidence sought found:** S02 abstract gives the interpretation; underlying examples were unavailable.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Cannot separate behavior, rhetoric and selection from the abstract.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** The study might also reflect changing enforcement, reporting vocabulary or later military developments.

**Inference limit:** Abstract-only access; no independently inspected beliefs, petitions or individual trajectory.

## POL-K05

Guan, Palma and Wu distinguish full silver convertibility (1260–76), nominal convertibility (1277–1309) and a fiat regime from 1310; exchange availability and official status were distinct.

**Cases:** POL-C02. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [POL-S04: The rise and fall of paper money in Yuan China, 1260–1368](https://www.hoover.org/sites/default/files/2025-01/Rise%20and%20Demise%20of%20Paper%20Money%20in%20Yuan%20China.pdf), pp. 15–18, Figure 2 and exchange-bureau discussion. Supports: Authors reconstruct regime stages and spatially unequal redemption access.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Description of reconstructed monetary configurations.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Exact transition boundaries vary by one year in parts of manuscript; local availability could diverge from legal promise.

**Inference limit:** Periodization is historians’ reconstruction; no uniform household access is inferred.

## POL-K06

The study’s reconstructed respectability CPI estimates annual inflation of 11.0% for 1260–1290, 1.8% for 1290–1340 and 4.5% for 1340–1355, contradicting uniform century-long hyperinflation.

**Cases:** POL-C02. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [POL-S04: The rise and fall of paper money in Yuan China, 1260–1368](https://www.hoover.org/sites/default/files/2025-01/Rise%20and%20Demise%20of%20Paper%20Money%20in%20Yuan%20China.pdf), pp. 26–28, Figure 6. Supports: Price basket, sparse observations, interpolation, and reported compound rates.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Reports the authors’ bounded calculation and its measurement method.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Individual prices and regions differed; the final collapse extends beyond 1355 price data.

**Inference limit:** About a dozen observations per commodity with interpolation/extrapolation; modelled index, not modern continuous official CPI. No household cost-of-living equivalence.

## POL-K07

In the reconstructed 1260–1355 series, civil-war frequency correlates with greater nominal and real note issuance; imperial grants do not show comparable statistical associations in Table 4.

**Cases:** POL-C02. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [POL-S04: The rise and fall of paper money in Yuan China, 1260–1368](https://www.hoover.org/sites/default/files/2025-01/Rise%20and%20Demise%20of%20Paper%20Money%20in%20Yuan%20China.pdf), pp. 43–48, equations 1–2 and Table 4. Supports: Civil-war coefficients and distinctions among nominal/real issues and rival demands.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Civil conflict -> fiscal demands/tax difficulty -> greater note issuance -> potential pressure on currency value.
- **strongest rival:** Inflation and collapsing revenue cause civil conflict; or imperial grants/disasters dominate spending.
- **discriminating observation:** Different associations for war types and grants, with temporally ordered independently measured fiscal shortages.
- **evidence sought found:** S04 Table 4 finds civil-war associations in nominal/real issues, no grant association, and weak real-disaster associations; only sparse fiscal totals.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Annual reconstructed time series does not remove reciprocal causation or omitted political changes; fiscal intermediate evidence incomplete.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Natural disasters weakly correlate with real issuance at the 10% level; external-war coefficients are not significant.

**Inference limit:** Political disorder, inflation and fiscal erosion can reinforce each other. No exogenous shock identifies the fiscal channel; absence of significance is not proof of no effect.

## POL-K08

Yuan legal monetary unification coexisted with regional and task-specific practices: Ichimaru reports different uses of guanwen and dingliang, while Guan et al. report silver property transactions despite official restrictions.

**Cases:** POL-C02. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [POL-S05: The distinctive uses of two monetary units, guanwen and dingliang, in Yuan China (1260–1368) as recorded in 'Heicheng chutuwenshu' and 'Huizhou qiyuewenshu'](https://www.jstage.jst.go.jp/article/sehs/68/3/68_KJ00004582583/_article/-char/en), English abstract. Supports: Regional account-unit and government/commercial distinctions.
- [POL-S04: The rise and fall of paper money in Yuan China, 1260–1368](https://www.hoover.org/sites/default/files/2025-01/Rise%20and%20Demise%20of%20Paper%20Money%20in%20Yuan%20China.pdf), p. 15, Table 2; p. 13 note 22. Supports: Private silver use and Yunnan cowry exception.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Separates observable institutional rules, units of account and reported practices.

**Counterevidence/rival:** A regional unit of account is not automatically a different circulating medium.

**Inference limit:** Contract-level evidence in S05 not inspected; formal legal-tender scope must not be equated with every transaction.

## POL-K09

Polo’s paper-money narrative combines threat of punishment for refusal with portability, official authentication and damaged-note replacement; it cannot independently establish willing universal acceptance.

**Cases:** POL-C02. **Evidence:** historical-primary. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [POL-S06: The Book of Ser Marco Polo, Book II, Part I, Chapter XXIV: paper currency](https://afe.easia.columbia.edu/mongols/figures/ser_xxiv.pdf), pp. 1–2, chapter XXIV. Supports: Narrative explicitly juxtaposes coercion and convenient transactions/redemption.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Source-critical description, not validation of every traveler assertion.

**Counterevidence/rival:** The narrative also insists on ready acceptance and enormous imperial riches; these are claims to assess, not measured outcomes.

**Inference limit:** Confidence is high about text content; the frequency of punishments, distribution of consent and national coverage are unknown.

## POL-K10

Late-Qing telegraph development included strategic state priorities and controlled private operation; the Gao–Lei sample records office access for 64 of 130 rice-reporting prefectures by 1904.

**Cases:** POL-C03. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [POL-S07: Communication Infrastructure and Stabilizing Food Prices: Evidence from the Telegraph Network in China](https://assets.aeaweb.org/asset-server/files/13141.pdf), pp. 7–9 and 12–13, sections I.A and II.B. Supports: Strategic line-building narrative; explicit sample treatment counts.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Bounded deployment description and denominator.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Routes could still reflect economic geography; state priorities do not prove random placement.

**Inference limit:** Sample is nine provinces, not all Qing territory; office presence does not measure affordability, uptime or individual usage.

## POL-K11

Yoon argues that shareholder-dividend priorities, underinvestment, high charges and technical/service problems limited the telegraph’s popular use even after network establishment.

**Cases:** POL-C03. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** low. **Causal status:** hypothesis.

- [POL-S08: Dashed Expectations: Limitations of the telegraphic service in the late Qing](https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/modern-asian-studies/article/abs/dashed-expectations-limitations-of-the-telegraphic-service-in-the-late-qing/62CACD9A5094EBEAFD5E0E760EB55C69), Abstract. Supports: Author links shareholder returns, equipment choices, pricing and limited market reach.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Dividend demands -> constrained equipment investment/high fees -> unreliable expensive service -> restricted public uptake.
- **strongest rival:** High capital costs, risk or low demand require high tariffs; technical failures precede rather than result from financing choices.
- **discriminating observation:** Investment decisions and dividend commitments preceding delays, with comparable capital-cost routes or customer elasticities.
- **evidence sought found:** S08 abstract supplies mechanism; full budgets and customer series unavailable. S07 finds beneficial price effects on a different margin.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Cannot choose among financing and demand mechanisms; do not generalize to all state/private ownership models.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Gao–Lei find price effects consistent with commercially useful communication; limited mass use is compatible with gains among traders.

**Inference limit:** Abstract-only; no audited tariff-demand series or proof that dividends, rather than scarce capital/risk, caused underinvestment.

## POL-K12

Zhou and Ding portray Sheng Xuanhuai as defending telegraph investors against court encroachment while also having entangled personal interests; the abstract records a proposal for state purchase instead of appropriation.

**Cases:** POL-C03. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [POL-S09: 盛宣怀与上海电报局改制 / Sheng Xuanhuai and the Systematic Reform of Shanghai Telegraph Bureau in the Late Qing Dynasty](https://qsyj.ruc.edu.cn/CN/Y2019/V0/I3/110), Chinese/English abstracts. Supports: Conflicted official/investor interests and purchase-versus-confiscation proposal.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Attributed description of actors and proposals, not a causal claim of modernization.

**Counterevidence/rival:** The Chinese abstract also notes Sheng sometimes pressured shareholders from the court’s side.

**Inference limit:** A proposal is not completed reform, public consultation or popular legitimacy; underlying correspondence not inspected.

## POL-K13

Gao and Lei’s difference-in-differences study estimates that telegraph office access reduced monthly maximum high-quality rice prices by about 4.2–5.5% of the sample mean in 130 prefectures over 1870–1904.

**Cases:** POL-C03. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** causal-identification.

- [POL-S07: Communication Infrastructure and Stabilizing Food Prices: Evidence from the Telegraph Network in China](https://assets.aeaweb.org/asset-server/files/13141.pdf), pp. 10–17, Table 3 and Figure 3. Supports: Data, specifications, effect magnitudes and pretrend checks.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Faster information across existing trade routes -> merchants adjust orders -> lower local price extremes.
- **strongest rival:** Coincident transport/institutional change, placement selection or altered reporting accounts for falling maxima.
- **discriminating observation:** Pretrends, transport/port controls, route/subsample tests and reactions to connected versus unconnected external shocks.
- **evidence sought found:** S07 checks pretrends, railway/treaty-port controls, excludes selected locations and tests weather/network channels.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Assumed June treatment month, spillovers, official reporting quality and staggered-treatment assumptions remain; no household outcomes.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Office placement was not randomized; reporting changes and time-varying conflicts could remain. Lower-quality rice estimates are smaller/less precise.

**Inference limit:** Outcome is reported monthly wholesale maxima, not average consumer expenditure, famine deaths or welfare; original regressions not replicated.

## POL-K14

The same study finds telegraph access dampened local-weather price effects but increased sensitivity to weather in connected regions; price volatility rose in previously stable markets and fell in previously volatile ones.

**Cases:** POL-C03. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [POL-S07: Communication Infrastructure and Stabilizing Food Prices: Evidence from the Telegraph Network in China](https://assets.aeaweb.org/asset-server/files/13141.pdf), sections IV–V, pp. 22–29. Supports: Weather-location interactions, disconnected-region placebo and volatility heterogeneity.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Connected price information -> trade arbitrage links markets -> local shocks spread -> less local isolation and redistributed risk.
- **strongest rival:** General administrative change reduces price volatility everywhere regardless of network.
- **discriminating observation:** Opposite responses to local and connected distant shocks; no similar response to disconnected shocks.
- **evidence sought found:** S07 reports these directional tests and heterogeneous volatility by baseline risk.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Trade flows and household welfare not directly measured; weight/model choices and common reporting may matter.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** The result is shared-risk transmission, not uniform stabilization; estimated mechanisms depend on exposure weights and reported weather.

**Inference limit:** Same sample as K13, not independent corroboration; no distribution of gains among net buyers, sellers or laborers.

## POL-K15

The 1812 imperial decree retained metric base units while ordering commercially familiar fractions/multiples and dual markings on instruments, and retained official metric teaching.

**Cases:** POL-C04. **Evidence:** historical-primary. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [POL-S10: Décret concernant l’uniformité des poids et mesures, 12 février 1812](https://annales.ensmp.fr/articles/1812-1/235-245.pdf), printed pp. 453–454, articles 1–6. Supports: Direct decree on unchanged units, adapted instruments and teaching.
- [POL-S11: Les origines du système métrique en France et la Convention du mètre de 1875, qui a ouvert la voie au Système international d’unités et à sa révision de 2018](https://comptes-rendus.academie-sciences.fr/physique/item/10.1016/j.crhy.2018.12.002.pdf), pp. 13–14. Supports: Retail implementation and legal continuity.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Direct legal configuration, carefully distinguished from compliance.

**Counterevidence/rival:** The resulting arrangements modified decimal practice; describing unchanged base units alone would hide a substantial compromise.

**Inference limit:** Law states what was authorized and intended, not how widely instruments were supplied or accepted.

## POL-K16

A material implementation gap accompanied early metric mandates: Treat reports that secondary standards had not been distributed when compulsory use was declared in 1795.

**Cases:** POL-C04. **Evidence:** institutional-synthesis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [POL-S13: A History of the Metric System Controversy in the United States](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/Legacy/SP/nbsspecialpublication345-10.pdf), p. 10, note 51. Supports: Secondary standards/commercial instrument shortfall.
- [POL-S12: Faites poids et mesures…](https://archives.isere.fr/sites/isere-archives-fr/files/inline-files/ca_13_poids_mesures_protege.pdf), dossier, PDF p. 6. Supports: Municipal standard provision and later conversion tables.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Mandate before local standards/instruments -> users lack calibrated practical implements -> legal uniformity fails to ensure comparable practice.
- **strongest rival:** Users had sufficient implements but deliberately resisted new measures.
- **discriminating observation:** Dated deliveries/orders compared with local compliance, including places supplied early.
- **evidence sought found:** S13 states missing secondary standards; S12 describes municipal supply and tables. No matched delivery/compliance panel inspected.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Physical and social obstacles could operate together; relative contributions unmeasured.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Familiarity, pricing interests and political disruption also constrained uptake; instruments alone would not ensure adoption.

**Inference limit:** Retrospective evidence does not estimate what share of delay was caused by supply constraints.

## POL-K17

The law of 4 July 1837 repealed the 1812 arrangement and prohibited nonmetric measures from 1 January 1840; this supplies a legal deadline, not a date of universal practical adoption.

**Cases:** POL-C04. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [POL-S11: Les origines du système métrique en France et la Convention du mètre de 1875, qui a ouvert la voie au Système international d’unités et à sa révision de 2018](https://comptes-rendus.academie-sciences.fr/physique/item/10.1016/j.crhy.2018.12.002.pdf), pp. 13–14, quoted articles 1 and 3. Supports: Repeal and effective date.
- [POL-S13: A History of the Metric System Controversy in the United States](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/Legacy/SP/nbsspecialpublication345-10.pdf), pp. 10–11, note 53. Supports: Translated prohibitions including commercial/legal settings.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Corroborated legal sequence; explicit non-equivalence to use.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Older words and task measures persisted according to the Isère synthesis.

**Inference limit:** Both accounts derive from the same law; original Bulletin des lois not inspected. No national compliance denominator.

## POL-K18

The Isère archival synthesis describes customary measures tied to agricultural work and continued rural usage beyond the 1840 legal deadline; practical task fit is a plausible part of persistence, not a measured universal preference.

**Cases:** POL-C04. **Evidence:** institutional-synthesis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [POL-S12: Faites poids et mesures…](https://archives.isere.fr/sites/isere-archives-fr/files/inline-files/ca_13_poids_mesures_protege.pdf), PDF pp. 2, 6–9, especially journal/land-work discussion and long-run coda. Supports: Task-linked area measures, local implementation and later coexistence.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Established work/commodity conventions -> customary units remain useful for local tasks -> continued mixed practice despite a legal standard.
- **strongest rival:** Pure inertia or educational exclusion, with no continuing practical utility.
- **discriminating observation:** Contexts where a task-linked measure persists as the task persists and changes when work changes, despite ability to use metric units.
- **evidence sought found:** S12 links the journal to horse-ploughing and describes later rural coexistence; no individual longitudinal literacy/use evidence.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Source offers qualitative association, not a discriminating controlled comparison.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Habit, limited schooling, fraud and political resistance may also explain use; the source does not isolate these channels.

**Inference limit:** Coda extends beyond 1795–1840 and is used only to reject instantaneous displacement. Neither continued terminology nor local examples establish unchanged quantities nationally.

## PSY-K01

In Busia’s research-assisted on-farm trials, half-teaspoon CAN top-dressing produced a mean calculated seasonal return of 36%, while the full Ministry-recommended fertilizer/hybrid-seed package had a negative mean return; higher physical yield did not make every input configuration profitable.

**Cases:** PSY-C01. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** causal-identification.

- [PSY-S02: How High Are Rates of Return to Fertilizer? Evidence from Field Experiments in Kenya](https://poverty-action.org/sites/default/files/publications/How%20High%20are%20Rates%20of%20Return%20to%20Fertilizer.pdf), pp.483–487; Table1, panels B/D. Supports: Randomised adjacent plots, dose-specific yield and calculated return comparisons.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Specified dose/package -> changed input application -> yield less input cost -> calculated return
- **strongest rival:** All fertilizer packages have uniformly high private returns, versus configuration-specific economics.
- **discriminating observation:** Compare net returns, not only yields, across randomised doses/packages.
- **evidence sought found:** PSY-S02 Table1: positive mean return at half teaspoon; negative at one teaspoon and full official package.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Labour/assistance, small-plot noise and heterogeneous future conditions limit economic transportability.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Small plots, assisted application and noisy seasonal returns; risk and labour assumptions affect interpretation.

**Inference limit:** Identifies tested plot-package performance under those conditions, not farm-wide realised profits or a profitable dose for every farmer.

## PSY-K02

Randomised harvest-time SAFI offers increased household fertilizer use in treated seasons (adjusted +14.3pp and +18.1pp in the two main seasons), but did not produce a detectable persistent increase after the offer ceased.

**Cases:** PSY-C01. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** causal-identification.

- [PSY-S01: Nudging Farmers to Use Fertilizer: Theory and Experimental Evidence from Kenya](https://www.atai-research.org/wp-content/uploads/2016/07/aer-Nudging-Fertilizer-2011.pdf), Table4 A/B; pp.2373–2375. Supports: Season-specific randomised offer effects and follow-up coefficients.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Early delivered purchase offer -> additional acquisition/use this season -> possible learning/wealth change -> later use
- **strongest rival:** One use permanently changes knowledge, wealth or habit enough to maintain increased adoption.
- **discriminating observation:** Positive later-season effects when original offer is absent.
- **evidence sought found:** PSY-S01 Table4 shows treatment-season gains and later estimates near comparison-group usage.
- **remaining ambiguity:** No detectable persistence does not prove no learning; liquidity, shocks or small scale may offset learned benefits.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** About one-quarter of originally sampled farmers lacked adoption data; full-control denominators smaller. Persistent effects could be smaller than study precision.

**Inference limit:** Household any-plot use, not stable individual habit, market-wide diffusion or sustained welfare gain.

## PSY-K03

SAFI’s pattern is consistent with procrastination/commitment mechanisms, but does not uniquely identify present bias: late free delivery estimated +9–10pp without significance, the early–late difference had p=.08, and timing-choice take-up did not fully match the simple model.

**Cases:** PSY-C01. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [PSY-S01: Nudging Farmers to Use Fertilizer: Theory and Experimental Evidence from Kenya](https://www.atai-research.org/wp-content/uploads/2016/07/aer-Nudging-Fertilizer-2011.pdf), pp.2375–2377, §VI pp.2383–2387; Table6. Supports: Timing arms, prechoice, maize-purchase test, pilot intention/purchase gaps and alternative explanations.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Harvest-time small acquisition-cost reduction -> earlier purchase/less delay -> more seasonal use
- **strongest rival:** Time-consistent farmers with seasonal resources, fixed costs and changing opportunities also prefer early offers.
- **discriminating observation:** Announce late offers in advance; vary liquidity independently; directly elicit intertemporal choices and relate them prospectively to response.
- **evidence sought found:** PSY-S01 includes timing prechoice, favourable maize-purchase test and smaller announced-delay pilots; these constrain some simple rivals, but p=.08 timing contrast and model misfit remain.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Several timing and saving motives share predictions; favourable maize sale is not a complete liquidity intervention.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Seasonal liquidity, opportunity costs, trusted delivery and a surprise late offer can also matter; pilots use purchases rather than subsequent use.

**Inference limit:** No independently elicited present-bias parameter. Cannot equate one insignificant arm with zero or read welfare rankings directly from trial outcomes.

## PSY-K04

Among 489 surveyed Indonesian seaweed growers, 86% could not state their current pod size and 87% could not state an optimal size, whereas most could state spacing; these beliefs were elicited before uptake outcomes and alongside observed farm practices.

**Cases:** PSY-C02. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [PSY-S03: Learning Through Noticing: Theory and Evidence from a Field Experiment](https://www.hbs.edu/ris/Publication%20Files/Learning%20Through%20Noticing%20Field%202014_c1214d62-9429-4357-b47a-4195dae00881.pdf), TableII and §IV.A, pp.1329–1331; field measurement §III. Supports: Input-specific knowledge responses and independently recorded plot practices.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Reports baseline answers and the measurement design without assigning a psychological cause.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Being unable to answer in grams may partly reflect measurement language rather than complete lack of practical knowledge.

**Inference limit:** A bounded descriptive contrast, not a universal cognitive deficit among farmers.

## PSY-K05

In the seaweed trial, input changes were more evident after personalised summaries than after trial participation alone; measured pod-size phase differences were significant, but the approximately 7g post-summary treatment effect itself was imprecise.

**Cases:** PSY-C02. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** causal-identification.

- [PSY-S03: Learning Through Noticing: Theory and Evidence from a Field Experiment](https://www.hbs.edu/ris/Publication%20Files/Learning%20Through%20Noticing%20Field%202014_c1214d62-9429-4357-b47a-4195dae00881.pdf), TableIV, pp.1337–1338; TableV. Supports: Post-summary self-reported change +0.162/+0.171; pod-size effects 6.951/7.316g; phase-difference p=.003/.015; fixed-effect post-summary p=.14.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** On-plot trial plus later interpreted results -> information usable for input choice -> subsequent pod-size/practice changes
- **strongest rival:** Experience alone eventually causes learning; later change need not arise specifically from noticing.
- **discriminating observation:** Separate trial-only, delayed-learning and summary-only arms at the same follow-up date, with objective practices.
- **evidence sought found:** PSY-S03 TableIV provides randomised treated/control contrasts at two stages and objective weights; phase differences for weights support a change after summaries.
- **remaining ambiguity:** No independent summary timing randomisation; direct effect precision is weak and farmer demand effects possible.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Self-reported phase-difference equality not rejected (p=.148/.221); spacing did not change. All treated farmers received summary later, rather than randomising explanation time separately.

**Inference limit:** Randomised trial-package versus control supports effects of exposure path, not a pure independently randomised summary-only effect or long-run income improvement.

## PSY-K06

Limited attention is a plausible account of the seaweed input-specific pattern, but explanation also reduces computational demands and can alter credibility; the evidence does not isolate attention as the sole mechanism.

**Cases:** PSY-C02. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [PSY-S03: Learning Through Noticing: Theory and Evidence from a Field Experiment](https://www.hbs.edu/ris/Publication%20Files/Learning%20Through%20Noticing%20Field%202014_c1214d62-9429-4357-b47a-4195dae00881.pdf), §IV.C, pp.1341–1342; footnote29; TablesII–V. Supports: Authors consider labour costs, numeracy and perceptibility; farmers knew a summary was coming.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Unnoticed input dimension -> raw experience not encoded -> salient explanation -> changed practice
- **strongest rival:** Summary makes arithmetic easier, provides trusted advice or simply arrives after enough time.
- **discriminating observation:** Vary salience while holding calculations, recommendation and timing fixed; independently measure attention/recall.
- **evidence sought found:** PSY-S03 baseline knowledge plus measurements fits attention; §IV.C explicitly discusses computational rival and footnote29 notes anticipated summaries.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Belief reports are stronger than inference from uptake alone but not direct attention measurement.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Baseline differences across input dimensions support attention; however, spacing and weight tasks need not impose the same calculation burden.

**Inference limit:** No laboratory findings borrowed from cited papers; neither an innate deficit nor irrationality established. The model itself allows costly attention to be chosen optimally given mistaken priors.

## PSY-K07

In the 120-village Malawi extension experiment, performance incentives to ordinary peer communicators increased their experimentation/communication and downstream adoption; simply appointing local communicators without incentives did not reliably produce that diffusion.

**Cases:** PSY-C03. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** causal-identification.

- [PSY-S05: Social Learning and Incentives for Experimentation and Communication](https://spinup-000d1a-wp-offload-media.s3.amazonaws.com/faculty/wp-content/uploads/sites/45/2021/11/Benyishay_Mobarak_Malawi.pdf), §§2.1–2.2,5–7; Tables5–7. Supports: Cross-randomised communicator/incentive design, knowledge exams and observed practices.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Performance reward -> communicator effort/own demonstration -> recipient knowledge -> farm adoption
- **strongest rival:** Passive diffusion follows automatically from selecting respected/local farmers, without effort incentives.
- **discriminating observation:** Cross-randomise rewards; observe communicator actions and recipient knowledge/practices rather than correlate friends’ uptake.
- **evidence sought found:** PSY-S05 documents weak unincentivised effects and stronger rewarded peer communication/adoption.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Payment, task salience, number of contacts and demonstrator adoption interact; identity-versus-number remains unresolved.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Peer strategy used five communicators versus one lead farmer; number, relevance and identity not separately randomised. Incentives can signal priorities as well as pay for effort.

**Inference limit:** Supports incentive-dependent communication in this institutional programme, not a universal claim that peers need payment or that ordinary identity alone causes adoption.

## PSY-K08

In the separate 200-village Malawi network experiment, central-seed targeting increased pit-planting diffusion relative to extension-worker selection; adoption-rate differences were significant in year two but not year three, and 45% of benchmark villages had no non-seed adopter after three years.

**Cases:** PSY-C03. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** causal-identification.

- [PSY-S04: Can Network Theory-based Targeting Increase Technology Adoption? (January 2021 author manuscript)](https://faculty.wcas.northwestern.edu/~lab823/BBMM_Jan2021.pdf), pp.5–6; §§2–4 and adoption tables. Supports: Same two seed slots across four selection arms; diffusion rates and any-non-seed village outcome.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Network-based choice of two trained seeds -> wider relevant contacts -> some non-seed adoption
- **strongest rival:** Any two locally selected informed farmers eventually yield equivalent reach over observed time.
- **discriminating observation:** Hold training/seed count fixed and randomise selection rule; compare both average adoption and stalls.
- **evidence sought found:** PSY-S04 reports ~3pp rate advantage in years2/3, precise only year2, and fewer no-diffusion villages under central targeting.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Three years cannot establish permanent stalls; centrality co-varies with other seed attributes.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Some benchmark villages did diffuse; most farmers still did not adopt even under central targeting. Network collection expensive; CRM differs from pit planting.

**Inference limit:** No necessity edge warranted. The larger village-level probability of any adoption and average individual uptake are different estimands.

## PSY-K09

Female-reserved communicator roles in the Malawi 143-village study elicited lower recipient ratings and lower training attendance despite comparable measured communicator knowledge/use; pooled downstream knowledge/adoption and yields did not show a precise corresponding gender penalty.

**Cases:** PSY-C03. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** causal-identification.

- [PSY-S06: Gender Gaps in Technology Diffusion (public author manuscript)](https://spinup-000d1a-wp-offload-media.s3.amazonaws.com/faculty/wp-content/uploads/sites/45/2019/10/GenderMalawi.pdf), Tables3–7, §§4.1–4.2.3, pp.14–21. Supports: Objective competence, recipient ratings, training attendance, knowledge/adoption/yields measured separately.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Reservation changes messenger composition -> recipient appraisal/contact -> possible learning/adoption change
- **strongest rival:** Lower reception simply reflects lower measured technical competence or necessarily creates lower final diffusion.
- **discriminating observation:** Compare objective communicator competence, independent recipient perceptions and downstream results within assignment arms.
- **evidence sought found:** PSY-S06 finds no measured learning gap, lower attendance/ratings, but weak pooled downstream penalties.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Selected communicators may differ on unmeasured teaching qualities; perception elicitation after assignment and heterogeneous subgroup results limit mediation claims.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Some female-recipient knowledge/adoption contrasts are negative at p<.10; pooled imprecision is not equivalence. Unreserved roles not exclusively male by design.

**Inference limit:** Identifies effects of reservation arrangements, not a biological sex effect or complete causal mediation through prejudice.

## PSY-K10

The Malawi network data support a multiple-contact learning interpretation, but complex contagion is not uniquely established: connectedness can change exposure, demonstration and persuasion as well as reinforcement of beliefs.

**Cases:** PSY-C03. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [PSY-S04: Can Network Theory-based Targeting Increase Technology Adoption? (January 2021 author manuscript)](https://faculty.wcas.northwestern.edu/~lab823/BBMM_Jan2021.pdf), §5 and pp.5–6; individual two-seed link analysis. Supports: Two-seed connections predict learning/adoption under randomised seed assignments; author explicitly notes alternatives.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Multiple informed connections -> reinforced useful information -> learning/adoption
- **strongest rival:** More contacts increase encounter probability, effort or demonstrator relevance without a discrete reinforcement threshold.
- **discriminating observation:** Hold contact time/content and seed effort constant while varying independent sources; measure belief updating before use.
- **evidence sought found:** PSY-S04 uses assignment-induced two-seed exposure and knowledge; PSY-S05 demonstrates that communication effort itself can respond to incentives.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Cross-study comparison suggests a rival rather than jointly identifying a mechanism; studies share institutions and researchers.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Larger effects where baseline knowledge low and land more suitable are consistent with learning; neither is exclusive to a threshold mechanism.

**Inference limit:** Do not turn network topology into a universal psychological threshold or infer that one contact never suffices.

## PSY-K11

In Humes et al.’s 154-completer trial, both effective audiology-fitted and consumer-selected preset aids improved major hearing-benefit measures versus placebo, while satisfaction and preliminary keep intention differed and daily logged use did not differ significantly across service arms.

**Cases:** PSY-C04. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** causal-identification.

- [PSY-S07: The Effects of Service-Delivery Model and Purchase Price on Hearing-Aid Outcomes in Older Adults: A Randomized Double-Blind Placebo-Controlled Clinical Trial](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5597084/), Results, Figures6–10; Table5; preliminary decision-to-keep section. Supports: Randomised service comparisons with several distinct outcomes; 81% versus55% versus36% preliminary keep intention.
- [PSY-S13: Erratum: Hearing-aid clinical trials and Connected Speech Test calibration](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6808309/), Full erratum, paragraphs1–3. Supports: CST test was at +1dB SNR, not originally reported +3dB.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Effective amplification plus specified delivery -> task benefit -> satisfaction/use/retention decisions
- **strongest rival:** Professional contact/placebo alone accounts for apparent hearing gains, or usage alone measures all benefit.
- **discriminating observation:** Same hardware with placebo programming and blinded outcome assessment; separate benefit, hours and choice.
- **evidence sought found:** PSY-S07 active models beat placebo on benefit, but usage fails to separate groups; S13 corrects test calibration.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Fitting, orientation and interpersonal service bundled; post-trial rescue fitting not independently randomised.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Selected volunteers, short period, no prior use and same hardware; absence of significance is not proof identical efficacy. Speech-test calibration corrected.

**Inference limit:** The intention percentages are not actual final retention rates. Consumer model simulated OTC access within a clinic; does not verify all OTC products or user groups.

## PSY-K12

The hearing-aid price comparison was not randomised: earlier participants paid $3,600 and later participants $600; the null price findings for many benefit measures and concentration of returns at the high price cannot identify a causal price effect.

**Cases:** PSY-C04. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [PSY-S07: The Effects of Service-Delivery Model and Purchase Price on Hearing-Aid Outcomes in Older Adults: A Randomized Double-Blind Placebo-Controlled Clinical Trial](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5597084/), Introduction final paragraphs; Methods Trial Design; final returns section. Supports: Sequential cohorts; 17/20 returners paid high price, versus60/134 keepers.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Methodological fact and observed cohort association; deliberately no causal claim about price.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Price cohorts were roughly balanced across service arms, but that does not randomise price or remove recruitment/time confounding.

**Inference limit:** Neither price irrelevance nor a quantified affordability effect inferred.

## PSY-K13

In Wallhagen’s 91 Bay Area help-seeking dyads, independently elicited accounts linked visible hearing aids with age, disability and perceived competence; some participants rejected or discounted stigma, especially in supportive relationships.

**Cases:** PSY-C04. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [PSY-S09: The Stigma of Hearing Loss](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC2904535/), Methods; Results: altered self-perception, ageism/vanity and supportive/nonsupportive others. Supports: Separate repeated interviews elicited meanings and reasons before/after choices, including contrary accounts.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Social meanings of visible aids -> anticipated social response/identity cost -> help-seeking or use decision
- **strongest rival:** Low experienced benefit/cost explains refusal, and identity narratives are post-hoc rationalisation.
- **discriminating observation:** Measure concerns before decisions and manipulate social framing/support while holding device benefit/access fixed.
- **evidence sought found:** PSY-S09 uses repeated separate interviews and follows decisions, including supportive and unconcerned participants; no framing experiment.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Narratives establish a plausible local mechanism but cannot estimate mediation or independently establish actual discrimination.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Self-reports may rationalise decisions, and stigma can coexist with costs and poor benefit. Participants already seeking assessment; predominantly White.

**Inference limit:** Evidence establishes heterogeneous expressed meanings, not universal innate preferences, a stigma prevalence estimate or a causal effect of de-stigmatisation.

## PSY-K14

In a separate earlier Indiana volunteer cohort, 105 of 139 analysed never-users purchased hearing aids and 21 purchasers returned them; returners had poorer measured aided outcomes, while nonpurchasers differed mainly in perceived difficulties and expectations.

**Cases:** PSY-C04. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [PSY-S08: Differences Between Older Adults Who Do and Do Not Try Hearing Aids and Between Those Who Keep and Return the Devices](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8182629/), Methods Participants; Abstract; Results adherent/nonadherent and keep/return comparisons. Supports: Analytic sample and outcome associations after controlling for hearing severity.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Original observational associations and counts; separate stages of adoption without a causal preference inference.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Only34 nonpurchasers returned for measurement; only 15 of 21 returners completed end-of-trial outcomes. Unmeasured confounding and selective participation.

**Inference limit:** These are research-volunteer analytic denominators, not population adoption/abandonment rates. No claim that beliefs cause purchase or poor benefit alone causes return.

## PSY-K15

For Bihar’s bicycle-grant exposure, the adjusted triple-difference estimate was +5.2pp in age-appropriate grade-9 enrolment/completion for girls, relative to older cohorts, boys and Jharkhand; it is not an estimate of actual cycling or total lifetime secondary enrolment.

**Cases:** PSY-C05. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** causal-identification.

- [PSY-S10: Cycling to School: Increasing Secondary School Enrollment for Girls in India](https://econweb.ucsd.edu/~kamurali/papers/Published%20Articles/Publisher%20Formatted%20Versions/Cycling%20to%20School%20%28AEJ%20Applied%202017%29.pdf), §§II–III; Table2 pp.333–334. Supports: Age14–15 versus16–17; female/male; Bihar/Jharkhand; full-controls N=30,112, coefficient0.052(SE0.025).

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Earmarked grant -> more feasible school access -> age-appropriate grade9 participation
- **strongest rival:** General Bihar reform or household/cohort composition would create similar measured change without bicycles.
- **discriminating observation:** Check gender/state/cohort pretrends, socioeconomic controls and ineligible-grade placebo.
- **evidence sought found:** PSY-S10 Tables1/2/8: triple-difference pretrend not rejected; grade8 placebo near zero; adjustment materially reduces estimate.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Tests cannot exclude all cohort-specific female reforms or ascertain individual bicycle use.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Estimate falls from0.103 without controls to0.052 with household controls; no randomised rollout. Concurrent state reforms remain a concern.

**Inference limit:** Causal interpretation conditional on difference-in-differences assumptions and age/cohort comparability.

## PSY-K16

Bihar’s distance heterogeneity and ineligible-grade placebo favour a school-access channel, but do not separate saved travel time, safety, peer travel and changed social norms.

**Cases:** PSY-C05. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [PSY-S10: Cycling to School: Increasing Secondary School Enrollment for Girls in India](https://econweb.ucsd.edu/~kamurali/papers/Published%20Articles/Publisher%20Formatted%20Versions/Cycling%20to%20School%20%28AEJ%20Applied%202017%29.pdf), Distance analysis §§II–III; Table8 pp.343–345; discussion pp.346–348. Supports: Effects concentrated farther from secondary schools; grade8 placebo0.001; norms/safety discussed as possible channels.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Grant-enabled riding -> lower distance/safety burden -> participation and possible peer/norm feedback
- **strongest rival:** Other reforms or a conditional transfer’s income/incentive effect generate enrolment without norm change.
- **discriminating observation:** Compare distances and noneligible grades; separately measure commute, danger and norms; randomise mobility versus unrestricted cash.
- **evidence sought found:** PSY-S10 distance gradient and grade8 placebo support access; no direct isolation of safety/norms or cash-only counterfactual.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Several pathways predict the same distance pattern; equilibrium social spillovers not independently identified.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Roads and law/order may complement bicycles even if they do not explain the whole effect; outcomes do not directly measure household norms.

**Inference limit:** Do not call Bihar a randomised norm-change intervention or treat enrolment as evidence of unrestricted mobility.

## PSY-K17

A later Bihar reanalysis finds stronger bicycle-programme estimates in villages with all-weather road connectivity, but its health-facility road proxy and nonrandom infrastructure do not establish that such roads are strictly necessary.

**Cases:** PSY-C05. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [PSY-S12: Infrastructure and Girls’ Education: Bicycles, Roads, and the Gender Education Gap in India](https://www.ifo.de/DocDL/wp-2022-382-seebacher-gender-education-gap-india.pdf), §2 pp.4–5; §3 pp.9–10; main results Tables3–4. Supports: DLHS health-facility access proxy, quadruple-difference estimates and authors’ unobserved-difference caution.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Usable roads plus bicycle access -> reliable travel -> school participation
- **strongest rival:** Road variable proxies wealth, governance, school access or treatment implementation rather than causal complementarity.
- **discriminating observation:** Actual school-route quality and exogenous road changes interacted with bicycle offers.
- **evidence sought found:** PSY-S12 uses health-facility road connectivity, controls and pretrend checks, finding heterogeneity; cannot observe precise school routes.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Insignificant disconnected estimate cannot distinguish absence from imprecision; no factorial road experiment.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** No-road indicator permits dry-season tracks; unobserved village differences and route quality remain. Same main survey/policy lineage as S10.

**Inference limit:** Contributory infrastructure hypothesis, not independent replication or bounded technical necessity.

## PSY-K18

In Zambia’s 100-school experiment, the bicycle-plus-support package reduced self-reported one-way commute by 35.08 minutes (control 103.77) and last-week absence by 0.29 days (control 1.01) at one year; the final article abstract rounds these to 34% and 29%.

**Cases:** PSY-C05. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** causal-identification.

- [PSY-S11: Wheels of Change: Transforming Girls’ Lives with Bicycles (7 May 2025 author manuscript)](https://www.nishithprakash.com/s/Wheels_of_Change.pdf), Tables1/4, pp.38/41; §§3–6; attrition §4.4.2. Supports: 25 payment,20 free,55 control schools; commute N=1,879; absence N=1,952; school-clustered randomisation inference.
- [PSY-S14: Wheels of Change: Transforming Girls’ Lives with Bicycles (final journal abstract and metadata)](https://doi.org/10.1093/ej/ueaf122), Final journal abstract. Supports: Rounded34% commute and29% absenteeism reductions; final full text not acquired.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Bike/support offer -> usable school transport -> shorter commute -> better attendance
- **strongest rival:** Common schooling improvement explains gains, or mere stated enthusiasm without travel/task change.
- **discriminating observation:** Randomised school control and concrete travel/attendance outcomes, with attrition checks and longer administrative follow-up.
- **evidence sought found:** PSY-S11 Tables1/4 show large specific effects; S14 abstract confirms rounded direction/magnitude; manuscript attrition bounds inspected.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Self-report not blinded; no independent randomisation of maintenance/custody; follow-up excludes some girls and later records are separately scoped.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Differential follow-up and self-report; programme includes mechanics, committees and use restrictions. One-year dropout/grade-transition/English effects not significant.

**Inference limit:** Effect of the installed package on eligible already-enrolled girls; not a bare-asset effect, school-entry effect or proof all girls can use bicycles.

## PSY-K19

Zambia’s nominal-fee versus free bicycle arms did not yield precise differences in first-stage transport or reported empowerment outcomes; a parental-payment/psychological-ownership explanation is therefore not identified by one arm being significant and the other not.

**Cases:** PSY-C05. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [PSY-S11: Wheels of Change: Transforming Girls’ Lives with Bicycles (7 May 2025 author manuscript)](https://www.nishithprakash.com/s/Wheels_of_Change.pdf), Tables1/3 pp.38/40; §6.3 pp.20–21. Supports: Formal fee/free equality p-values: commute .40; aggregate empowerment .13; agency subscales .11–.99.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Parental fee -> signal of investment/psychological ownership -> greater agency or sustained use
- **strongest rival:** Free ownership supports agency equally, or physical access dominates; higher fee-arm point estimates are sampling variation.
- **discriminating observation:** Test fee-versus-free contrasts directly and measure ownership/investment beliefs before outcomes.
- **evidence sought found:** PSY-S11 reports no precise between-arm differences on these outcomes; older abstract’s stronger fee narrative is not adopted.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Only one nominal fee and restricted setting; cannot rule out smaller effects, selective reporting or other fee sizes.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Some fee-arm point estimates higher; trial is not a powered equivalence/noninferiority design, and nearly universal uptake limits selection tests.

**Inference limit:** No general conclusion that charging is harmless, superior or irrelevant for poorer noneligible populations.

## PSY-K20

In Zambia’s May2025 manuscript, the package improved selected self-reported agency measures, notably locus of control and bargaining, but general mobility and pooled aspirations were imprecise; aggregate empowerment varies with specification/correction. The final abstract says educational aspirations improved, an unresolved difference from the inspected pooled tables. The inspected evidence does not identify a distinct norm-change mechanism.

**Cases:** PSY-C05. **Evidence:** experiment. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** causal-identification.

- [PSY-S11: Wheels of Change: Transforming Girls’ Lives with Bicycles (7 May 2025 author manuscript)](https://www.nishithprakash.com/s/Wheels_of_Change.pdf), Table3 p.40; TableA.11 p.56; §6.3; §4.4.3 p.16. Supports: Main locus-control0.18SD/bargaining0.21SD; controlled appendix0.16/0.19 with corrected p-values .02/.04 and <=.01. Pooled aspirations0.07(SE0.05) in Table3 and0.06(SE0.05), corrected p=.44/.50 in TableA.11. Behavioural games discontinued.
- [PSY-S14: Wheels of Change: Transforming Girls’ Lives with Bicycles (final journal abstract and metadata)](https://doi.org/10.1093/ej/ueaf122), Final journal abstract, aspirations sentence. Supports: States that educational aspirations improved; final tables/outcome definitions and pooled-versus-arm-specific interpretation not inspected. This records the version discrepancy, not corroboration of a positive pooled effect.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Supported school transport -> changed daily constraints/experience -> selected reported agency dimensions
- **strongest rival:** Interview demand, asset salience or family reallocation changes reports without broad social norms changing.
- **discriminating observation:** Independent behavioural or longitudinal agency measures and a design separating response salience/demand from durable control; pre/post questionnaire measurement and multiple-testing corrections alone cannot discriminate this rival.
- **evidence sought found:** PSY-S11 randomised survey-index contrasts and appendix corrections support some reported dimensions; they do not distinguish the stated response mechanism rival. Behavioural games were discontinued (§4.4.3) and supply no confirmation. S14 aspirations assertion remains unresolved against manuscript pooled tables.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Mechanism among saved time, safety, ownership, school exposure and survey response changes remains underdetermined; self-report not blind.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Aggregate index p-value .04 or .12 across adjustment families in manuscript appendix; no direct normative mediator randomised. Final abstract’s positive aspirations assertion is not reconciled with manuscript pooled estimates; no inference that final tables changed. Asset custody rules constrain movement and resale.

**Inference limit:** Causal identification is confined to the randomised package’s effects on the measured questionnaire indices in the inspected manuscript. Demand/salience versus deeper agency or norm change is not separated. These are neither unrestricted freedom nor adult earnings/occupation outcomes; final full-text reconciliation remains open.

## SKL-K01

After H4's sea trials, the Board commissioned Kendall to copy it; K1's 1769 completion and Cook-voyage operation provide a named replication by another maker, not evidence of economical serial manufacture.

**Cases:** SKL-C01. **Evidence:** institutional-synthesis. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [SKL-S01: K1 — Marine timekeeper / watch (ZAA0038), Board of Longitude Explorers](https://www.rmg.co.uk/sites/default/files/import/media/pdf/K1.pdf), Description. Supports: 1766 commission, 1769 completion and second Cook voyage use.
- [SKL-S02: Larcum Kendall — obituary, Gentleman's Magazine 60(2), pp. 1213–1214 (1790), transcribed in Cook's Log 40(2), p. 42 (2017)](https://www.captaincooksociety.com/cooks-life/people/cooks-officers-and-crew-and-contemporaries/larcum-kendall-obituary), Opening copying-contract paragraph. Supports: Contemporary account presents the commission as a test of another maker's execution.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Identifies a particular copy and trial, without assigning a causal effect to commission funding.

**Counterevidence/rival:** The obituary's approximate delivery date differs; RMG's completion date is used, with the disagreement preserved.

**Inference limit:** One copy establishes neither typical manufacturing yield nor fleet-wide access.

## SKL-K02

Kendall's obituary reports cleaning K1 in 1775 and, after deterioration and stoppage on Cook's third voyage, replacing some corroded parts; a working precision artifact therefore did not remove the need for service during its useful life.

**Cases:** SKL-C01. **Evidence:** historical-primary. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [SKL-S02: Larcum Kendall — obituary, Gentleman's Magazine 60(2), pp. 1213–1214 (1790), transcribed in Cook's Log 40(2), p. 42 (2017)](https://www.captaincooksociety.com/cooks-life/people/cooks-officers-and-crew-and-contemporaries/larcum-kendall-obituary), 1775 cleaning and third-voyage paragraphs. Supports: Reports disassembly/cleaning, altered rate, stoppage and rust requiring new parts.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Long voyage exposure/operation -> rate change and deterioration -> service and part replacement -> repair work on a still-valued instrument.
- **strongest rival:** Exceptional mistreatment, not an inherent design burden, may explain this breakdown.
- **discriminating observation:** Comparable watches with documented handling/cleaning and rate histories would separate design vulnerability from an exceptional service episode.
- **evidence sought found:** S02 explicitly records both successful voyages and later corrosion; no matched watch/handling records inspected.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Shows actual maintenance work, but not why that amount was needed or how representative it was.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** The account is laudatory and cannot establish whether contamination, handling, design or another cause produced the failure.

**Inference limit:** No breakdown probability or comparative maintenance cost is inferred.

## SKL-K03

In the late eighteenth-century navigation system described by Higgitt, marine timekeepers and astronomical observations were used together; chronometer deployment did not make local-time observation or independent checks dispensable.

**Cases:** SKL-C01. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.
