- [SKL-S03: Longitude](https://ethos.lps.library.cmu.edu/article/id/451/), Investing in longitude and Navigation systems, notes 24 and 28–33. Supports: Joint trials, local time comparison, calibration and continued astronomical practice.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** evaluated
- **mechanism chain:** Portable reference time + observed local time/rate checks -> usable longitude comparison -> complementary instrument/observer work.
- **strongest rival:** A simple replacement account predicts astronomy becomes unnecessary when a good watch arrives.
- **discriminating observation:** Named joint use and the need to identify/restore a disturbed reference would contradict complete replacement.
- **evidence sought found:** S03 describes combined Cook trials, calibration and local-time measurements; mathematical comparison also requires both reference and local information.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Chronometer convenience may reduce some lunar work; complementarity does not imply equal effort or unchanged practice.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Relative reliance on methods differed by voyage and task; ordinary dead reckoning persisted.

**Inference limit:** No claim that every navigator made every observation or that one method was always best.

## SKL-K04

Japanese clockmakers served seasonal-hour conventions through different mechanisms, including alternating foliots and movable hour markers; many required periodic human adjustment rather than adoption of equal-hour time.

**Cases:** SKL-C02. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [SKL-S04: Hisashige Tanaka and His Myriad Year Clock](https://utcp.c.u-tokyo.ac.jp/publications/pdf/CollectionUTCP6_Hashimoto_02.pdf), pp. 36–37. Supports: Explains alternating foliots, marker adjustment and two automatic exceptions.
- [SKL-S05: What is a Traditional Japanese Clock (Wadokei)?](https://museum.seiko.co.jp/en/knowledge/relation_15/), Opening explanation. Supports: Defines seasonal-hour system and recurring adjustment.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Describes documented mechanism configurations and operator tasks, not the cause or prevalence of their adoption.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Surviving examples overrepresent durable/collectable artifacts; local calendars and practice were not necessarily uniform.

**Inference limit:** Technical alternatives do not establish nationwide household ownership or punctuality.

## SKL-K05

Tanaka's 1851 Myriad Year Clock automated seasonal markers, yet Hashimoto reports only one completed clock and no buyer despite an intention to sell more; exceptional mechanical elaboration did not itself establish a scalable product.

**Cases:** SKL-C02. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [SKL-S04: Hisashige Tanaka and His Myriad Year Clock](https://utcp.c.u-tokyo.ac.jp/publications/pdf/CollectionUTCP6_Hashimoto_02.pdf), pp. 37–41. Supports: Disassembly account, complex mechanism and unsold single-clock history; price-refusal story is explicitly anecdotal.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Elaborate automatically adjusting design -> costly/complex individual artifact -> intended elite sale fails -> no observed repeated production.
- **strongest rival:** A career change or limited sales opportunity, rather than excessive complexity, explains the singular output.
- **discriminating observation:** Workshop accounts, offers, orders and later reproduction attempts could distinguish price/production constraints from changed priorities.
- **evidence sought found:** S04 supplies artifact study and biographical sales narrative, but not accounts or a reproducibility trial by another maker.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Single output is better established than its cause; complexity is not treated as a sufficient explanation.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Tanaka subsequently changed work under patronage; opportunity costs and lack of marketing may rival price/complexity. The purchaser story is not an account ledger.

**Inference limit:** No calculated profitability, buyer willingness-to-pay or typical Japanese manufacturing limit.

## SKL-K06

Tanaka's documented career combined commercial craft work, instruction in calendrical theory/practice from the Tsuchimikado family in 1847, and later Saga patronage; the exact tools used to make his clock's fine components remain uncertain.

**Cases:** SKL-C02. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [SKL-S04: Hisashige Tanaka and His Myriad Year Clock](https://utcp.c.u-tokyo.ac.jp/publications/pdf/CollectionUTCP6_Hashimoto_02.pdf), pp. 33–34,40. Supports: Shop/patronage sequence, specialist instruction and explicit uncertainty about clockmaking tools.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Records known learning/work settings and a material knowledge gap; makes no necessity claim for tacit transmission.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Career reconstruction relies partly on biographies and selected surviving records; some youthful-inventor anecdotes are unreliable.

**Inference limit:** This is a named route to expertise, not proof of the necessary educational sequence or relative contributions of teaching and self-practice.

## SKL-K07

Hall's 1811 rifle patent preceded his 1819 government contract and years of tooling; his and Talcott's statements describe reduced dependence on handwork, not the elimination of skilled toolmaking or measured gains for workers.

**Cases:** SKL-C03. **Evidence:** institutional-synthesis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [SKL-S07: John H Hall](https://home.nps.gov/hafe/learn/historyculture/john-h-hall.htm), Contract paragraph and 1822/1832 testimony. Supports: Separates patent, procurement, tooling and contemporaries' assertions about ordinary labour/machinery.
- [SKL-S06: Archeological Investigation of the Armory Street, Lower Armory Grounds, Harpers Ferry Armory (46JF518)](https://npshistory.com/series/archeology/ncr/21/report.pdf), p. 55. Supports: Main factory's piece-rate and 55-task organisation already existed by 1816.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Sequences institutional events and identifies the limits of contemporary labour assertions.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Hall was defending his project, and the museum page is celebratory; no independent cost or skill inventory is provided.

**Inference limit:** Cannot infer net deskilling, lower full cost or higher wages from promoters' statements.

## SKL-K08

The Harpers Ferry standardisation account describes working gauges checked against a separate gauge set, so repeated dimensions were supported by maintained measurement references as well as machines; the later HAFE7427 set documents physical inspection equipment.

**Cases:** SKL-C03. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [SKL-S06: Archeological Investigation of the Armory Street, Lower Armory Grounds, Harpers Ferry Armory (46JF518)](https://npshistory.com/series/archeology/ncr/21/report.pdf), p. 59, notes 244–245. Supports: 1823 gauge adoption and separate gauges to check working gauges.
- [SKL-S12: Box of U.S. Rifle Model 1841 Inspection Gauges (View 4), HAFE 7427](https://artsandculture.google.com/asset/view-4/KAFQGvXgiHqy_Q?childAssetId=XgHi054cXIxrog), Catalog HAFE7427, ca.1846. Supports: Surviving Model 1841 inspection set attributed to Thornton; same set as S06 figure 19.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Component variation -> comparison with gauges -> checking working gauges against references -> a repeatable basis for accepting dimensions.
- **strongest rival:** Machines alone deliver sufficient repeatability, with inspection merely ceremonial.
- **discriminating observation:** Documented checks on the gauges themselves are more expected when measurement drift matters than when machine output is self-validating.
- **evidence sought found:** S06 explicitly describes reference checking; S12 supplies a later physical set but no actual pass/fail dataset.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Operational importance is supported; marginal effects on yield/cost remain unidentified.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** A gauge can wear or be used incorrectly; its existence alone does not show output conformed. Different model and workshop dates cannot be combined into one universal system.

**Inference limit:** No tolerance values, rejection rates or universal necessity of this exact gauge system are claimed.

## SKL-K09

A1829 examination of Harpers Ferry Model 1816 muskets made in 1819–1820 still found incomplete interchangeability, according to the NPS report's Schmidt-based account; Hall's rifle-work claims cannot be applied to every armory model.

**Cases:** SKL-C03. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [SKL-S06: Archeological Investigation of the Armory Street, Lower Armory Grounds, Harpers Ferry Armory (46JF518)](https://npshistory.com/series/archeology/ncr/21/report.pdf), p. 56, note 232. Supports: Identifies inspected model/production dates and reports good overall quality with defects and incomplete interchangeability.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** A bounded negative observation that limits the population to which a success claim can apply.

**Counterevidence/rival:** The original inspection report was not acquired; the report does not supply a component pass-rate denominator here.

**Inference limit:** Different product designs and factories prevent this from being a matched causal comparison.

## SKL-K10

In Grace's garage evidence, novices moved through supervised routine maintenance, physical assistance and more specialised repair; apprenticeship exchanged labour for instruction and small shares of profits, and included cleaning/cooking or errands alongside mechanical tasks.

**Cases:** SKL-C04. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [SKL-S08: African Motors: Technology, Gender, and the History of Development](https://www.bibliopen.org/p/bopen/9781478094456), pp. 94–95 (method),118–121 (apprenticeship). Supports: Direct observation and oral histories of allocation of tasks, supervision, support and gradual access to specialised work.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Describes witnessed and reported training practices, distinguishing later observation from historical recollection.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Interview narratives stress intelligence and omit routine work; author participation helps reveal this but was short and not representative.

**Inference limit:** No typical learning duration, graduation probability or monetary return is established.

## SKL-K11

Personal tool accumulation could widen apprentices' ability to take work outside a garage; Grace records salvaged tools and one mechanic's growth from an under-tree shop in 1967 to larger premises and welding equipment in 1972.

**Cases:** SKL-C04. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [SKL-S08: African Motors: Technology, Gender, and the History of Development](https://www.bibliopen.org/p/bopen/9781478094456), p. 120, notes 141–142. Supports: Links own wrenches/tools to independent work and provides Juma Katigiro's dated account.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Participation -> accumulation of usable tools and skill -> ability to accept some independent jobs -> possible workshop autonomy.
- **strongest rival:** Demand/reputation alone drives both tool purchases and independent work; tools may proxy success.
- **discriminating observation:** Track otherwise comparable apprentices before and after obtaining tools, including those who never exit and job types needing particular equipment.
- **evidence sought found:** S08 links particular tools to tasks and narrates one expansion, but gives no apprentice cohort or exogenous tool access.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Tools have a concrete task role; their marginal contribution to entry or income remains unknown.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Customer connections, skill and demand grow together with tools; failed entrants are poorly observed.

**Inference limit:** One career and qualitative accounts do not identify an asset effect or prove tools alone suffice.

## SKL-K12

Grace records both hand-based and machine-assisted automotive repair, and disputes over their reliability; some mechanics preferred stock modules, manuals or model specialisation over extensive improvisation.

**Cases:** SKL-C04. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [SKL-S08: African Motors: Technology, Gender, and the History of Development](https://www.bibliopen.org/p/bopen/9781478094456), pp. 136–142, table 2.1. Supports: Regional machine access, hand refinishing, contested quality, modular reliability, manuals and specialisation.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Changing parts/machine access -> choice among manual fabrication, reuse or stock-module repair -> continued service with variable effort/reliability.
- **strongest rival:** Regional mechanical cultures or reputation strategies, rather than access alone, explain method differences.
- **discriminating observation:** Compare like repairs with measured equipment/parts availability, labour time and subsequent failure under similar loads.
- **evidence sought found:** S08 describes alternative methods and explicit disagreement, including preference for original modules; no comparative lifetime tests found.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Access, skill, culture and vehicle mix vary jointly; no universal superiority of either ingenuity or standard replacement.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Mechanics' reputational criticism may be self-serving; observed continued use does not establish comparative safety or long life.

**Inference limit:** Repair-based alternatives are real, but their desirability cannot be ranked without costs, workloads, durability and user risks.

## SKL-K13

Most technicians in Mchome's interview sample said they had formal vocational training and also acquired experience through informal practice after graduation; the evidence does not support an exclusive formal-versus-informal skills dichotomy.

**Cases:** SKL-C05. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [SKL-S09: Resilience from Below: Technicians, Repair and Maintenance Works in Post-socialist Dar es Salaam, 1985–2020](https://reference-global.com/pdf/10.2478/host-2022-0005), pp. 78,83–85. Supports: Interview method and technicians' reports of combined learning routes.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Reports a defined interview finding and its sample limit, not an effect of training on repair performance.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Educational statistics are expressly unavailable; formal-training claims are self-reported and the sample is nonrepresentative.

**Inference limit:** No estimate of the added value of either training route or national technician composition.

## SKL-K14

Dar es Salaam users in Mchome's study reported continuing to use repaired appliances, including one television four years after repair; component repair offered a route to retained service alongside replacement or going without.

**Cases:** SKL-C05. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [SKL-S09: Resilience from Below: Technicians, Repair and Maintenance Works in Post-socialist Dar es Salaam, 1985–2020](https://reference-global.com/pdf/10.2478/host-2022-0005), pp. 85–87; interview52. Supports: Customer report of television repair and continued use; description of power-supply/component work.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Damaged appliance + constrained replacement budget -> paid repair/component work -> reported restored and continued service.
- **strongest rival:** Only easily repaired devices/successful customers entered the interview record; durable outcomes reflect selection rather than general repair effectiveness.
- **discriminating observation:** Follow all attempted repairs, including failures, and compare service life/cost/safety with matched replacements and unrepaired devices.
- **evidence sought found:** S09 includes a concrete four-year user report and descriptions of interventions, but lacks a repair cohort or controlled tests.
- **remaining ambiguity:** The existence of a successful route is supported; its frequency, comparative durability and net welfare effect remain unresolved.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Failed repairs, safety problems and dissatisfied customers may be underrepresented; technician explanations of voltage incompatibility were not independently tested.

**Inference limit:** No citywide resilience effect, failure rate or engineering approval of the modifications is implied.

## SKL-K15

Mchome proposes that1990s utility retrenchment helped move trained personnel into street electrical work, but the inspected account cannot separate computerisation/prepaid metering from simultaneous structural-adjustment job cuts or measure workers' subsequent livelihoods.

**Cases:** SKL-C05. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** hypothesis.

- [SKL-S09: Resilience from Below: Technicians, Repair and Maintenance Works in Post-socialist Dar es Salaam, 1985–2020](https://reference-global.com/pdf/10.2478/host-2022-0005), pp. 91–93. Supports: Tanesco training history, restructuring, retirement/freelance pathway and temporary-work networks.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Utility reorganisation/technology/policy change -> lower-tier workforce reduction -> some trained workers sell outside services -> enlarged informal labour supply.
- **strongest rival:** Fiscal retrenchment independently causes job loss, while rising appliance imports/demand independently attracts freelancers.
- **discriminating observation:** Personnel records linking tasks, dismissal dates, technology rollout and post-exit earnings could distinguish the pathways.
- **evidence sought found:** S09 describes all these coincident changes and links some retired workers to freelance work; such personnel/earnings data were not inspected.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Supply and demand changes, causal order and magnitude remain unresolved.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Much of the account inherits other studies; no matched staff cohorts or complete employment sequence is provided.

**Inference limit:** A plausible occupational transition is not evidence that technology alone caused job loss or that informal work compensated for it.

## SKL-K16

Among the early TEA-laser laboratories Collins investigated, written-only attempts had not succeeded and working copies were associated with contact with successful builders; visits sometimes failed, and the author explicitly did not claim written-only success was impossible.

**Cases:** SKL-C06. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [SKL-S10: The TEA Set: Tacit Knowledge and Scientific Networks](https://research.engineering.nyu.edu/~jbain/scitechsoc/readings/74Collins.pdf), pp. 172–173,176–177,182–183. Supports: Network scope/search checks, successes/failures, contact routes and explicit generalisation caveat.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Early incomplete instructions + uncertain implementation -> visits/calls/placements -> additional practical information/experience -> working copies in observed network.
- **strongest rival:** Successful labs have greater prior expertise/resources, or merely receive missing explicit details; either can produce the same contact-success association.
- **discriminating observation:** Comparable labs assigned complete validated instructions, components and contact conditions, or documented later successful independent builds, would discriminate stronger tacit necessity.
- **evidence sought found:** S10 searched for isolates through three supplementary routes and reports no written-only successes, but also failed contacts and improving instructions in p. 176 note 26.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Contact packages expertise, disclosure, material advice and selection; these are not separately identified.
- **hypothesis timing:** pre-search candidate

**Counterevidence/rival:** Contact is selected, not random; early papers often omitted details deliberately, and unseen isolated successes might remain.

**Inference limit:** Supports a bounded role for contact, not an indispensable invisible ingredient in every technology or permanent impossibility of codification.

## SKL-K17

For some double-discharge TEA builds, demanding electrode-profile tolerances obstructed one laboratory while roughly filed profiles and aluminium banister rail worked elsewhere; the episode limits a general 'more precision solves replication' explanation.

**Cases:** SKL-C06. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [SKL-S10: The TEA Set: Tacit Knowledge and Scientific Networks](https://research.engineering.nyu.edu/~jbain/scitechsoc/readings/74Collins.pdf), p. 177. Supports: Contrasts insuperable machining requirements with two reported successful rougher alternatives.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Uncertain design significance -> overly strict interpreted machining demand -> blocked build; alternative builders use looser geometry -> operation in those configurations.
- **strongest rival:** Strict geometry is necessary in the blocked lab's other configuration, while alternative circuitry permits rougher parts.
- **discriminating observation:** Hold the rest of the configuration fixed while varying verified electrode geometry and measuring output/reliability.
- **evidence sought found:** S10 reports the striking contrast but no controlled common-configuration test.
- **remaining ambiguity:** The reported rougher alternatives reject a categorical all-configurations strict-precision requirement. They do not distinguish the named configuration-specific rival; no controlled common-configuration substitution or reliability comparison is reported.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Other circuit and geometry differences may explain cross-laboratory outcomes; not all TEA designs share one tolerance envelope.

**Inference limit:** No numerical engineering tolerance or claim that precision is useless; these are historical observations, not construction instructions.

## SKL-K18

Accessible footnotes to Collins and Harrison's1975 follow-up record simultaneous changes to two build variables and repeated power-unit failures; those observations leave any exclusive knowledge-transfer explanation of that episode's delays underdetermined.

**Cases:** SKL-C06. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** mechanism-supported.

- [SKL-S11: Building a TEA Laser: The Caprices of Communication](https://journals.sagepub.com/doi/10.1177/030631277500500404), Footnotes2,8,10–11. Supports: Two variables changed together; teaching burden; at least six power-unit failures byJune1975; available written advice was not always consulted.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Build difficulties -> simultaneous adjustments and repairs amid time constraints -> eventual observations cannot isolate each input's effect.
- **strongest rival:** Learning alone explains progress; hardware failures are consequences of inexperienced construction rather than independent causes.
- **discriminating observation:** Contemporaneous logs and one-variable interventions distinguishing component faults, operator experience and available time.
- **evidence sought found:** S11 footnotes explicitly state the simultaneous changes, teaching load and repeated failures; no complete log/body text inspected.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Reciprocal causation between learning and hardware failures remains possible.
- **hypothesis timing:** emerged during research

**Counterevidence/rival:** Full narrative was not acquired, so the total chronology or authors' full causal explanation is not reconstructed.

**Inference limit:** Hardware reliability and time allocation are demonstrated candidate confounders, not measured shares of delay.

## SYN-K01

A use-centred historical research design includes persistence, coexistence, repair and alternatives that invention-centred timelines can omit.

**Cases:** method/synthesis. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [SYN-S01: The Shock of the Old: Technology and Global History since 1900 — Introduction (inspected excerpt)](https://sidoli.w.waseda.jp/Edgerton_2008_01.pdf), Introduction pp. xi–xv. Supports: Author explicitly argues for technology-in-use as an alternative historiographical focus.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Claim describes an author’s research design or explicit argument, not an identified adoption mechanism.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Invention-focused work remains useful for questions about novelty; this is a design argument, not a quantified bias estimate.

**Inference limit:** Does not validate each historical example in the book.

## SYN-K02

Comin and Mestieri distinguish estimated adoption timing from intensity of technology use; their growth accounting is model-based and uses a selected 25-technology, 139-country sample.

**Cases:** method/synthesis. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** high. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [SYN-S02: If Technology Has Arrived Everywhere, Why Has Income Diverged?](https://dcomin.host.dartmouth.edu/Publications_files/CM_rev_71917.pdf), Introduction pp.1–3; §4.1 p.18. Supports: Defines two diffusion margins, sample and modelling approach.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Claim describes an author’s research design or explicit argument, not an identified adoption mechanism.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Their representativeness assumption is not independently established by this programme.

**Inference limit:** Does not establish why diffusion differs or warrant exporting estimated lags to all technologies.

## SYN-K03

Rosenberg explicitly treats diffusion as necessary to understanding the economic consequences of technological change rather than equating invention with those consequences.

**Cases:** method/synthesis. **Evidence:** scholarly-analysis. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [SYN-S03: Factors affecting the diffusion of technology](https://www.cambridge.org/core/books/abs/perspectives-on-technology/factors-affecting-the-diffusion-of-technology/C129CAACB1FEFC00B840505B2C20D8D5), Publisher summary. Supports: Summary states why adoption and displacement rates matter.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** descriptive-only
- **reason:** Claim describes an author’s research design or explicit argument, not an identified adoption mechanism.

**Counterevidence/rival:** Detailed chapter was not inspected.

**Inference limit:** A methodological proposition, not evidence for a particular adoption mechanism.

## SYN-K10

The evidence observes different stages: a described principle, household availability, delivered equipment, measured use and beneficial outcomes are not interchangeable. Hero’s source silence is an observation limit, not an observed failure to deploy.

**Cases:** AGR-C07; ANC-C01; DIG-C04; PSY-C04. **Evidence:** synthesis-inference. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [ANC-S01: The Pneumatics of Hero of Alexandria from the original Greek](https://www.gutenberg.org/files/77400/77400-h/77400-h.htm), §50, p. 72; preface pp. vii–viii. Supports: Bounded evidence in ANC-K01: Mechanism and editorial framing of the inspected edition.
- [DIG-S07: Annual Status of Education Report (Rural) 2021](https://img.asercentre.org/docs/aser2021forweb.pdf), Table 10 p.28; Table 12 p.31. Supports: Bounded evidence in DIG-K13: Conditional access shares.
- [AGR-S12: Up in Smoke: The Influence of Household Behavior on the Long-Run Impact of Improved Cooking Stoves (2012 working paper)](https://www.hks.harvard.edu/sites/default/files/centers/cid/files/publications/faculty-working-papers/241_HannaDufloGreenstone.pdf), sections V–VII; discussion and conclusion, printed pp. 27–32. Supports: Bounded evidence in AGR-K18: Usage, maintenance, fuel, time, health and interpretation of the real programme's performance.
- [AGR-S16: Up in Smoke: The Influence of Household Behavior on the Long-Run Impact of Improved Cooking Stoves (published article)](https://www.povertyactionlab.org/sites/default/files/research-paper/52%20Up%20in%20Smoke%20AEJ2016.pdf), published pp.86–87,106–108. Supports: Bounded evidence in AGR-K18: Training and local promoters were present; repair/rebuilding campaign was not independently randomized.
- [PSY-S07: The Effects of Service-Delivery Model and Purchase Price on Hearing-Aid Outcomes in Older Adults: A Randomized Double-Blind Placebo-Controlled Clinical Trial](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5597084/), Results, Figures6–10; Table5; preliminary decision-to-keep section. Supports: Bounded evidence in PSY-K11: Randomised service comparisons with several distinct outcomes; 81% versus55% versus36% preliminary keep intention.
- [PSY-S13: Erratum: Hearing-aid clinical trials and Connected Speech Test calibration](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6808309/), Full erratum, paragraphs1–3. Supports: Bounded evidence in PSY-K11: CST test was at +1dB SNR, not originally reported +3dB.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Stage and observation definition -> different permissible inference; no causal pathway is identified by this distinction.
- **strongest rival:** Apparent stage differences may reflect missing records or differing measurement rather than failed use; where failure is observed, design, demand, exposure and operating work may each matter.
- **discriminating observation:** Separate records that do not observe an outcome from studies that directly measure use and benefit; compare within the same intervention before inferring a failed link.
- **evidence sought found:** Compared case-level claims ANC-K01, DIG-K13, AGR-K18, PSY-K11. Hero’s passage lacks productive-load observation. The stove follow-up directly records changing use/condition and health outcomes. The hearing-aid trial measures benefit, logged use and keep intention separately.
- **remaining ambiguity:** This is an evidence classification, not a test selecting maintenance, access or any general adoption mechanism. Missing ancient deployment remains unknown.
- **hypothesis timing:** retrospective synthesis; targeted revision after independent first-round criticism

**Counterevidence/rival:** Apparent stage differences may reflect missing records or differing measurement rather than failed use; where failure is observed, design, demand, exposure and operating work may each matter.

**Inference limit:** This is an evidence classification, not a test selecting maintenance, access or any general adoption mechanism. Missing ancient deployment remains unknown.

## SYN-K11

Observed material trade-offs, task-specific printing, hybrid textile inputs and lead-acid use reject automatic replacement by a newer technique. They do not distinguish freely chosen task fit from binding price, access or institutional constraints in every setting.

**Cases:** CTX-C01; ENE-C05; IND-C07; MAT-C05; NET-C02; SKL-C02. **Evidence:** synthesis-inference. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [MAT-S08: Arsenic Bronze: Dirty Copper or Chosen Alloy? A View from the Americas](https://www.academia.edu/31356952/Lechtman_Arsenic_Bronze_Dirty_Copper_1996), pp. 500–508; Tables 4–6; Figs. 25–27. Supports: Bounded evidence in MAT-K15: Mechanical tests and comparison with thin-sheet fabrication traditions.
- [NET-S05: Introduction, The Book Worlds of East Asia and Europe, 1450–1850: Connections and Comparisons](https://hkupress.hku.hk/image/catalog/pdf-preview/9789888208081.pdf), pp. 11–14, Technology Transfer. Supports: Bounded evidence in NET-K07: Division of labour, later reprinting from blocks, paper/premises and financing comparisons.
- [NET-S05: Introduction, The Book Worlds of East Asia and Europe, 1450–1850: Connections and Comparisons](https://hkupress.hku.hk/image/catalog/pdf-preview/9789888208081.pdf), pp. 14–15, especially discussion and n. 29. Supports: Bounded evidence in NET-K08: Woodblock predominance alongside explicitly identified movable-type niches.
- [NET-S04: A Selection of Shen Gua's Writings in Translation, appendix 3 to Shen Gua's Empiricism](https://asiacenter.harvard.edu/sites/default/files/2023-06/shengua_app3.pdf), pp. 23–24 and n. 71. Supports: Bounded evidence in NET-K08: Early process testimony and later predominance distinction.
- [NET-S16: The Great Divergence and the Economics of Printing](https://eprints.gla.ac.uk/120520/13/120520.pdf), January 2016 manuscript pp.17–22. Supports: Bounded evidence in NET-K08: Cost-model compatibility with the same genealogical and imperial niches.
- [IND-S15: Women’s Work and the Politics of Homespun in Socialist China, 1949–1980](https://www.cambridge.org/core/services/aop-cambridge-core/content/view/1B4DC63727682F1EEC958A62A4126757/S0020859012000521a.pdf/div-class-title-womenandapos-s-work-and-the-politics-of-homespun-in-socialist-china-1949-1980-div.pdf), pp. 373–374, notes 23–27. Supports: Bounded evidence in IND-K20: Describes mixed yarn use, Dingxian and geographical expansion of hand weaving.
- [ENE-S12: The future of electric two-wheelers and electric vehicles in China](https://escholarship.org/content/qt0d05f8v9/qt0d05f8v9.pdf), §2.1.1, pp. 2547–2548. Supports: Bounded evidence in ENE-K16: VRLA dominance and limited advanced-battery availability; export mix uncertainty.
- [ENE-S13: Comparative environmental impacts of electric bikes in China](https://itspubs.ucdavis.edu/download_pdf.php?id=1289), Conclusion, p. 289. Supports: Bounded evidence in ENE-K16: Approximate power-equivalent fourfold purchase price and two-to-threefold lifespan comparison.
- [SKL-S04: Hisashige Tanaka and His Myriad Year Clock](https://utcp.c.u-tokyo.ac.jp/publications/pdf/CollectionUTCP6_Hashimoto_02.pdf), pp. 37–41. Supports: Bounded evidence in SKL-K05: Disassembly account, complex mechanism and unsold single-clock history; price-refusal story is explicitly anecdotal.
- [CTX-S01: Women’s Work and the Politics of Homespun in Socialist China, 1949–1980](https://www.cambridge.org/core/services/aop-cambridge-core/content/view/1B4DC63727682F1EEC958A62A4126757/S0020859012000521a.pdf/womens-work-and-the-politics-of-homespun-in-socialist-china-1949-1980.pdf), p.377 for 1954 procurement/rationing; pp.386–387 for Zhouzhi use and cash. Supports: Bounded evidence in CTX-K02: Distinguishes coupon access from buying cloth and reports resale, household resources and exceptions.
- [CTX-S01: Women’s Work and the Politics of Homespun in Socialist China, 1949–1980](https://www.cambridge.org/core/services/aop-cambridge-core/content/view/1B4DC63727682F1EEC958A62A4126757/S0020859012000521a.pdf/womens-work-and-the-politics-of-homespun-in-socialist-china-1949-1980.pdf), pp.385–389, especially pp.388–389 and notes79–82. Supports: Bounded evidence in CTX-K03: Connects collective work, family expectations, blame, night work and recalled exhaustion.
- [CTX-S01: Women’s Work and the Politics of Homespun in Socialist China, 1949–1980](https://www.cambridge.org/core/services/aop-cambridge-core/content/view/1B4DC63727682F1EEC958A62A4126757/S0020859012000521a.pdf/womens-work-and-the-politics-of-homespun-in-socialist-china-1949-1980.pdf), pp.383–384 for exchanges and grave clothes; p.386 and note76 for interview-based cloth needs; p.382 captions for later niche. Supports: Bounded evidence in CTX-K04: Documents differentiated cloth services and an author estimate reflecting reported wear.
- [CTX-S01: Women’s Work and the Politics of Homespun in Socialist China, 1949–1980](https://www.cambridge.org/core/services/aop-cambridge-core/content/view/1B4DC63727682F1EEC958A62A4126757/S0020859012000521a.pdf/womens-work-and-the-politics-of-homespun-in-socialist-china-1949-1980.pdf), pp.373–374 compared with pp.386–389. Supports: Bounded evidence in CTX-K07: Contrasts historical background on hybrid inputs with original local findings on access and competing labour demands.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Different properties, tasks and feasible alternatives -> potential selective use or combination; the cause of each observed persistence must be tested separately.
- **strongest rival:** Persistence can follow cash scarcity, restricted access, customary obligation or script-sensitive costs even when a newer technique would be preferred under different constraints.
- **discriminating observation:** Observe feasible alternatives, prices, rights and comparable tasks; test whether changes in those constraints alter use without changing the technical properties.
- **evidence sought found:** Compared case-level claims MAT-K15, NET-K07, NET-K08, IND-K20, ENE-K16, SKL-K05, CTX-K02, CTX-K03, CTX-K04, CTX-K07. The printing niche observations also fit Angeles’s script-sensitive cost model; they refute impossibility, not that realistic rival. Lead-acid’s upfront cost contrast and the unsold elaborate clock constrain a simple performance hierarchy. The separately dated Guanzhong case observes cloth-coupon resale, cash scarcity and unpaid textile work alongside durability/social uses; this is not a backward projection into the pre-1949 episode.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Historical motive shares are unresolved; model fit and reused niche observations are not independent confirmations. No optimality or consent follows from coexistence.
- **hypothesis timing:** retrospective synthesis; targeted revision after independent first-round criticism

**Counterevidence/rival:** Persistence can follow cash scarcity, restricted access, customary obligation or script-sensitive costs even when a newer technique would be preferred under different constraints.

**Inference limit:** Historical motive shares are unresolved; model fit and reused niche observations are not independent confirmations. No optimality or consent follows from coexistence.

## SYN-K12

Documented output rights, project sanctions, enslaved maintenance, provider incentives and customary governance require separate choosers, payers, operators and affected nonchoosers. Uptake cannot serve as their shared welfare measure.

**Cases:** AGR-C03; ANC-C03; GHA-C02; GHA-C03; MED-C08; OCE-C02; OCE-C03. **Evidence:** synthesis-inference. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [AGR-S05: Contract Farming and Female Rice Growers in The Gambia](https://media.odi.org/documents/8169.pdf), sections 3–5, pp. 4–10. Supports: Bounded evidence in AGR-K07: Pre-project rights, project allocation, reclassification and household labour obligations.
- [AGR-S05: Contract Farming and Female Rice Growers in The Gambia](https://media.odi.org/documents/8169.pdf), Network Paper15 printed pp.5–6, section3. Supports: Bounded evidence in AGR-K25: Repayment condition, threat of usufruct loss, dated seizures and negotiated conditional return.
- [ANC-S05: The Aqueducts of Rome (De aquaeductu urbis Romae), Book II](https://penelope.uchicago.edu/Thayer/E/Roman/Texts/Frontinus/De_Aquis/Bennett/2%2A.html), §§116–117. Supports: Bounded evidence in ANC-K08: Reported headcounts, ownership and functions.
- [MED-S15: Addressing Antibiotic Abuse in China: An Experimental Audit Study](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4776334/), §I results; §III experiment 1 protocol. Supports: Bounded evidence in MED-K22: Randomly assigned standardized-patient roles and reported encounter rates.
- [OCE-S05: Budj Bim Cultural Landscape: World Heritage Nomination](https://whc.unesco.org/document/166283), pp.68–69. Supports: Bounded evidence in OCE-K06: Opposition, drainage, short water retention and traps rarely functioning outside floods.
- [OCE-S06: Lake Condah Restoration Project](https://www.gunditjmirring.com/lake-condah-restoration-project), opening project-history paragraphs. Supports: Bounded evidence in OCE-K06: Drain completion in 1954 and later restoration attempts.
- [OCE-S08: Aboriginal hunting buffers climate-driven fire-size variability in Australia’s spinifex grasslands](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3387077/), Materials and Methods, paragraphs on social/economic disincentives and kanyinin/Jukurrpa. Supports: Bounded evidence in OCE-K20: Ethnographic account of rights, costs, responsibilities and public-good recognition.
- [GHA-S04: From Forced to Voluntary Labour in Rural Africa: The Transition to Paid Voluntary Labour on the Roads of the Northern Territories of the Gold Coast](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0020859022000657), “The Transition from Paid Forced Labour to Paid Voluntary Labour on PWD Roads”, notes 50–52. Supports: Bounded evidence in GHA-K06: Protest, commissioners’ response and dated Savelugu diary on recruitment.
- [GHA-S03: Forced Labour, Roads, and Chiefs: The Implementation of the ILO Forced Labour Convention in the Gold Coast](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0020859018000524), pp. 464–471, especially p. 469 n. 89 and pp. 470–471. Supports: Bounded evidence in GHA-K08: Administrative powers, subsidy assumption, official dissent and local wage-payment initiatives.
- [GHA-S04: From Forced to Voluntary Labour in Rural Africa: The Transition to Paid Voluntary Labour on the Roads of the Northern Territories of the Gold Coast](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0020859022000657), “Indirect Rule, Taxation, and Unpaid Forced Labour”, notes 69–95. Supports: Bounded evidence in GHA-K08: Explicit engagement with Kunkel, divergent district practices, budgets, grants and later legislation.
- [GHA-S04: From Forced to Voluntary Labour in Rural Africa: The Transition to Paid Voluntary Labour on the Roads of the Northern Territories of the Gold Coast](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0020859022000657), “People’s Roads and Unpaid Voluntary Labour”, notes 100–109. Supports: Bounded evidence in GHA-K09: Bajamse request, village-built connections, grants and requests for tools/bridges.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Rights, sanctions and revenue can alter different actors’ choices; observed institutions establish actor differences without identifying one general institutional effect.
- **strongest rival:** Some divergent outcomes may reflect ecology, service quality or task differences alongside authority and incentives; recorded rules do not by themselves measure enforcement or individual welfare.
- **discriminating observation:** Trace the actual authority, sanction, payment and refusal options; use incentive changes where available, and measure outcomes for each affected group.
- **evidence sought found:** Compared case-level claims AGR-K07, AGR-K25, ANC-K08, MED-K22, OCE-K06, OCE-K20, GHA-K06, GHA-K08, GHA-K09. Carney records dated arrears and plot seizures with conditional return; Tae Rak documents opposed drainage; the prescribing audit changes expected dispensing location but retains distrust and design limitations. Northern Ghana adds competing accounts of paid recruitment, residual compulsion and farmer-initiated unpaid roads; administrative pay categories alone do not measure refusal.
- **remaining ambiguity:** The cases are not matched institutional treatments. A local prescribing contrast does not supply the causal effect of all institutions or a complete welfare balance.
- **hypothesis timing:** retrospective synthesis; targeted revision after independent first-round criticism

**Counterevidence/rival:** Some divergent outcomes may reflect ecology, service quality or task differences alongside authority and incentives; recorded rules do not by themselves measure enforcement or individual welfare.

**Inference limit:** The cases are not matched institutional treatments. A local prescribing contrast does not supply the causal effect of all institutions or a complete welfare balance.

## SYN-K13

Recurring work and inputs are documented in operating systems, but this corpus does not identify maintenance as the unique cause of persistence or reversal. Supported use and lower exposure can also coexist with a null clinical endpoint.

**Cases:** AGR-C07; ANC-C02; GHA-C03; MED-C06; SKL-C01. **Evidence:** synthesis-inference. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** hypothesis.

- [AGR-S12: Up in Smoke: The Influence of Household Behavior on the Long-Run Impact of Improved Cooking Stoves (2012 working paper)](https://www.hks.harvard.edu/sites/default/files/centers/cid/files/publications/faculty-working-papers/241_HannaDufloGreenstone.pdf), sections V–VII; discussion and conclusion, printed pp. 27–32. Supports: Bounded evidence in AGR-K18: Usage, maintenance, fuel, time, health and interpretation of the real programme's performance.
- [AGR-S16: Up in Smoke: The Influence of Household Behavior on the Long-Run Impact of Improved Cooking Stoves (published article)](https://www.povertyactionlab.org/sites/default/files/research-paper/52%20Up%20in%20Smoke%20AEJ2016.pdf), published pp.86–87,106–108. Supports: Bounded evidence in AGR-K18: Training and local promoters were present; repair/rebuilding campaign was not independently randomized.
- [AGR-S13: Liquefied Petroleum Gas or Biomass for Cooking and Effects on Birth Weight](https://stacks.cdc.gov/view/cdc/124935/cdc_124935_DS1.pdf), author-manuscript pp. 6–11; Methods, adherence and exposure Results. Supports: Bounded evidence in AGR-K19: Randomisation, intervention support, measured adherence and personal exposure contrast.
- [AGR-S13: Liquefied Petroleum Gas or Biomass for Cooking and Effects on Birth Weight](https://stacks.cdc.gov/view/cdc/124935/cdc_124935_DS1.pdf), author-manuscript Results and Discussion, pp. 8–11. Supports: Bounded evidence in AGR-K20: Adjusted birth-weight estimate, confidence interval and outcome-specific discussion.
- [ANC-S04: Operation and decline of the Barbegal mill complex, the largest industrial complex of antiquity](https://doi.org/10.1002/gea.22016), §5 discussion of flumes A–C, Fig. 9; §6 Conclusions, pp. 604–607. Supports: Bounded evidence in ANC-K06: Different terminal sequences and continued water flow.
- [MED-S11: Analysis of Historical Trends and Recent Elimination of Malaria from Sri Lanka and Its Applicability for Malaria Control in Other Countries](https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/public-health/articles/10.3389/fpubh.2017.00212/full), Malaria in Sri Lanka During the Second Half of the Twentieth Century. Supports: Bounded evidence in MED-K15: Counts and programme sequence.
- [MED-S12: Malaria resurgence: a systematic review and assessment of its causes](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1186/1475-2875-11-122), Weakening of the malaria control programme, Sri Lanka paragraph. Supports: Bounded evidence in MED-K15: Same sequence with surveillance and population-movement qualifications.
- [MED-S11: Analysis of Historical Trends and Recent Elimination of Malaria from Sri Lanka and Its Applicability for Malaria Control in Other Countries](https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/public-health/articles/10.3389/fpubh.2017.00212/full), Second Half of the Twentieth Century. Supports: Bounded evidence in MED-K16: 1964 consolidation, later resurgence, 1969 resistance and 1977 replacement.
- [MED-S12: Malaria resurgence: a systematic review and assessment of its causes](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1186/1475-2875-11-122), Sri Lanka example and Technical problems. Supports: Bounded evidence in MED-K16: Multiple operational and ecological causes.
- [SKL-S02: Larcum Kendall — obituary, Gentleman's Magazine 60(2), pp. 1213–1214 (1790), transcribed in Cook's Log 40(2), p. 42 (2017)](https://www.captaincooksociety.com/cooks-life/people/cooks-officers-and-crew-and-contemporaries/larcum-kendall-obituary), 1775 cleaning and third-voyage paragraphs. Supports: Bounded evidence in SKL-K02: Reports disassembly/cleaning, altered rate, stoppage and rust requiring new parts.
- [GHA-S04: From Forced to Voluntary Labour in Rural Africa: The Transition to Paid Voluntary Labour on the Roads of the Northern Territories of the Gold Coast](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0020859022000657), “People’s Roads and Unpaid Voluntary Labour”, note 110 and surrounding discussion. Supports: Bounded evidence in GHA-K10: Dated export interruption and reported road deterioration.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Hypothesised: provision/repair -> functioning use -> exposure or other service -> downstream outcome; each arrow may fail and feedback from demand may alter provision.
- **strongest rival:** Design, task fit, ecology, exposure outside the intervention, changed alternatives or reverse causation from declining demand may explain the outcome.
- **discriminating observation:** Define adequate provision before observing outcomes; trace condition, use, exposure and endpoints separately, with dated demand/design/ecology contrasts.
- **evidence sought found:** Compared case-level claims AGR-K18, AGR-K19, AGR-K20, ANC-K06, MED-K15, MED-K16, SKL-K02, GHA-K10. The stove package included training/promoters yet use declined; HAPIN’s supported use/exposure contrast limits a low-use explanation of every health null. Chronometer service is directly documented; malaria resistance detection does not date onset. The dated feeder-road deterioration after fuel/export interruption is compatible with demand affecting maintenance, but does not isolate that arrow.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Stove mediators remain bundled; no maintenance adequacy threshold inferred from failure is accepted. No universal necessity or effect size follows.
- **hypothesis timing:** retrospective synthesis; targeted revision after independent first-round criticism

**Counterevidence/rival:** Design, task fit, ecology, exposure outside the intervention, changed alternatives or reverse causation from declining demand may explain the outcome.

**Inference limit:** Stove mediators remain bundled; no maintenance adequacy threshold inferred from failure is accepted. No universal necessity or effect size follows.

## SYN-K14

Risk-contingent offers, full production cycles, complete journeys and input configurations change what is being compared. In the Ghana experiment, insurance-only offers increased investment and cash-only aggregate responses were smaller under the tested contracts; this does not rank constraints generally.

- [AGR-S02: Energetic efficiency and political expediency in Titicaca Basin raised field agriculture](https://www.academia.edu/6455173/Energetic_Efficiency_and_Political_Expediency_in_Titicaca_Basin_Raised_Field_Agriculture), pp. 280–288. Supports: Bounded evidence in AGR-K03: Energy/labour accounting, rotation comparisons, heterogeneous reported yields and political-economic interpretations.
- [MOB-S02: The costs of transporting goods by different modes: A case study of pottery movement in late Roman Britain](https://api.repository.cam.ac.uk/server/api/core/bitstreams/7291f9ad-f581-44cb-9e21-a63889ffd40b/content), §1.1 pp. 1–2 and §4.4 pp. 8–9. - [MOB-S01: ORBIS: The Stanford Geospatial Network Model of the Roman World, version dated 2 May 2012](https://orbis.stanford.edu/orbis2012/ORBIS_v1paper_20120501.pdf), p. 7 and p. 17. - [PSY-S02: How High Are Rates of Return to Fertilizer? Evidence from Field Experiments in Kenya](https://poverty-action.org/sites/default/files/publications/How%20High%20are%20Rates%20of%20Return%20to%20Fertilizer.pdf), pp.483–487; Table1, panels B/D. Supports: Bounded evidence in PSY-K01: Randomised adjacent plots, dose-specific yield and calculated return comparisons.
- [PSY-S01: Nudging Farmers to Use Fertilizer: Theory and Experimental Evidence from Kenya](https://www.atai-research.org/wp-content/uploads/2016/07/aer-Nudging-Fertilizer-2011.pdf), pp.2375–2377, §VI pp.2383–2387; Table6. Supports: Bounded evidence in PSY-K03: Timing arms, prechoice, maize-purchase test, pilot intention/purchase gaps and alternative explanations.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** State-contingent risk relief or different timing/input configuration -> changed feasible action/payoff -> observed investment or service comparison.
- **strongest rival:** Liquidity, anticipated future cash needs, treatment value, delivery timing and task-specific costs may contribute alongside risk or psychological mechanisms.
- **discriminating observation:** Vary the specified contract/input/timing before the outcome; compare equal-valued alternatives where possible and retain cash responses for particular inputs.
- **evidence sought found:** Compared case-level claims AGR-K15, AGR-K03, MOB-K03, PSY-K01, PSY-K03. Ghana supplies a randomized local investment contrast; Kenya supplies profitable small-dose trials and offer timing effects without uniquely identifying present bias. The ancient transport and agricultural comparisons have weaker identification.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Heterogeneous outcomes and treatment sizes cannot be pooled into a global ranking of risk, price or convenience. Original identification assumptions remain.
- **hypothesis timing:** retrospective synthesis; targeted revision after independent first-round criticism

**Counterevidence/rival:** Liquidity, anticipated future cash needs, treatment value, delivery timing and task-specific costs may contribute alongside risk or psychological mechanisms.

**Inference limit:** Heterogeneous outcomes and treatment sizes cannot be pooled into a global ranking of risk, price or convenience. Original identification assumptions remain.

## SYN-K16

A useful task, entry to its work and capture of its returns are distinct. Incumbent telephone operators’ losses coexisted with no measured overall employment reduction for subsequent young female cohorts; maintenance entry was gendered, while other histories show negotiated compensation, tool barriers or unpaid participation.

**Cases:** CTX-C01; GHA-C01; LBR-C01; MOB-C06; OCE-C01; SKL-C04. **Evidence:** synthesis-inference. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

- [LBR-S02: Answering the Call of Automation: How the Labor Market Adjusted to Mechanizing Telephone Operation](https://www.dpgross.com/papers/answering-call-automation.pdf), §VI, §VII and conclusion p.1934. Supports: Bounded evidence in LBR-K02: Linked incumbent outcomes differ from repeated-cohort employment findings.
- [LBR-S02: Answering the Call of Automation: How the Labor Market Adjusted to Mechanizing Telephone Operation](https://www.dpgross.com/papers/answering-call-automation.pdf), pp.1928–1929, direct labour-demand discussion and FigureV. Supports: Bounded evidence in LBR-K08: Gender composition of technicians and distinct successive-cohort test of complementary telephone employment.
- [MOB-S12: Volkswagenwerk Aktiengesellschaft v. Federal Maritime Commission, 390 U.S. 261](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/390/261), opening paragraphs 2–4, 390 U.S. 261. Supports: Bounded evidence in MOB-K22: Agreement, fund, and weight-versus-volume assessment dispute.
- [MOB-S13: California Cartage Co. v. NLRB, 822 F.2d 1203](https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/appellate-courts/F2/822/1203/373792/), part I, paragraphs describing break-bulk, 1960 agreement and off-dock container work. Supports: Bounded evidence in MOB-K23: Court recounts Board findings of greater-than-expected work loss and the dispute over a fifty-mile work-preservation agreement.
- [SKL-S08: African Motors: Technology, Gender, and the History of Development](https://www.bibliopen.org/p/bopen/9781478094456), pp. 94–95 (method),118–121 (apprenticeship). Supports: Bounded evidence in SKL-K10: Direct observation and oral histories of allocation of tasks, supervision, support and gradual access to specialised work.
- [SKL-S08: African Motors: Technology, Gender, and the History of Development](https://www.bibliopen.org/p/bopen/9781478094456), p. 120, notes 141–142. Supports: Bounded evidence in SKL-K11: Links own wrenches/tools to independent work and provides Juma Katigiro's dated account.
- [OCE-S14: He Pukoa Kani ‘Āina: Kanaka Maoli approaches to mo‘okū‘auhau as methodology](https://manoa.hawaii.edu/nhpol/wp-content/uploads/2022/05/8_Wilson-Hokowhitu-2012-p.-137-143.pdf), pp.143–145. Supports: Bounded evidence in OCE-K04: Interview method, participants’ choice about genealogy, volunteering and expressed motivations.
- [CTX-S01: Women’s Work and the Politics of Homespun in Socialist China, 1949–1980](https://www.cambridge.org/core/services/aop-cambridge-core/content/view/1B4DC63727682F1EEC958A62A4126757/S0020859012000521a.pdf/womens-work-and-the-politics-of-homespun-in-socialist-china-1949-1980.pdf), pp.373–374 compared with pp.386–389. Supports: Bounded evidence in CTX-K07: Contrasts historical background on hybrid inputs with original local findings on access and competing labour demands.
- [GHA-S01: Labour and land in Ghana, 1874–1939: a shifting ratio and an institutional revolution](https://eprints.lse.ac.uk/4392/1/Labour_and_land_in_Ghana_(LSERO_version).pdf), manuscript p. 7, notes 38–40. Supports: Bounded evidence in GHA-K03: Chiefly fleets, smaller owners and 1927–28 administrative attribution of larger firms’ retreat.
- [GHA-S02: The Development of Motor Transport in the Gold Coast, 1900–39](https://www.academia.edu/80974990/The_Development_of_Motor_Transport_in_the_Gold_Coast_1900_39), pp. 34–35. Supports: Bounded evidence in GHA-K03: Hire-purchase, owner-driver business, long workdays and contemporary profit descriptions.
- [GHA-S01: Labour and land in Ghana, 1874–1939: a shifting ratio and an institutional revolution](https://eprints.lse.ac.uk/4392/1/Labour_and_land_in_Ghana_(LSERO_version).pdf), manuscript pp. 17–20, especially p. 18 n. 104 and p. 20 n. 115. Supports: Bounded evidence in GHA-K05: Early bound labour in Ashanti cocoa planting, later hiring/pledging, road compulsion, migration and gender qualifications.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Task reallocation -> heterogeneous exposure, entry rights and bargaining -> potentially divergent livelihoods; this is not a demonstrated pathway into good work for all displaced people.
- **strongest rival:** Local labour demand, linked-sample selection and institutions governing entry/compensation may explain heterogeneous outcomes without any universal human comparative advantage.
- **discriminating observation:** Follow the same workers, measure entry costs and net returns, and compare entrants with incumbents and excluded people; do not substitute later cohorts for individual recovery.
- **evidence sought found:** Compared case-level claims LBR-K02, LBR-K08, MOB-K22, MOB-K23, SKL-K10, SKL-K11, OCE-K04, CTX-K07, GHA-K03, GHA-K05. Telephone study and bargaining records observe different units; tool accumulation and unpaid voyaging demonstrate concrete access/pay distinctions without wage forecasts. Guanzhong unpaid work and Ghanaian asset/debt/labour conditions add concrete access and value-capture limits; no displaced-carrier transition panel is observed.
- **remaining ambiguity:** No contemporary role passes a demand, access and value-capture validation gate in this edition; work may be automated, unpaid or inaccessible.
- **hypothesis timing:** retrospective synthesis; targeted revision after independent first-round criticism

**Counterevidence/rival:** Local labour demand, linked-sample selection and institutions governing entry/compensation may explain heterogeneous outcomes without any universal human comparative advantage.

**Inference limit:** No contemporary role passes a demand, access and value-capture validation gate in this edition; work may be automated, unpaid or inaccessible.

## SYN-K17

Measurement and design boundaries materially change interpretation: UK VR gaming reports, ORT episode definitions, container estimates, completed overnight reactor costs and sequential hearing-aid prices cannot be read as comparable adoption or welfare effects.

- [MOB-S11: Estimating the effects of the container revolution on world trade](https://qn1-next.xuetangonline.com/16800654811573.pdf), p. 37; Table 2 pp. 43–44; §3.4 pp. 45–46. - [ENE-S04: Historical construction costs of global nuclear power reactors](https://core.ac.uk/download/pdf/82188133.pdf), §3.2, pp. 374–375 and §5.2, p. 381. Supports: Bounded evidence in ENE-K08: Explicit exclusion of IDC; distinction from electricity cost and coverage frame.
- [ENE-S06: Sources of Cost Overrun in Nuclear Power Plant Construction Call for a New Approach to Engineering Design](https://dspace.mit.edu/bitstream/handle/1721.1/133049/Jacopo%27s%20and%20Jessika%27s%20paper%20on%20nuclear%20cost%20Sep%202020.pdf), Introduction, manuscript p. 1. Supports: Bounded evidence in ENE-K08: Reports 2017 abandonment of VC Summer as a separate project outcome.
- [PSY-S07: The Effects of Service-Delivery Model and Purchase Price on Hearing-Aid Outcomes in Older Adults: A Randomized Double-Blind Placebo-Controlled Clinical Trial](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5597084/), Introduction final paragraphs; Methods Trial Design; final returns section. Supports: Bounded evidence in PSY-K12: Sequential cohorts; 17/20 returners paid high price, versus60/134 keepers.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Observation population/denominator/version/design -> reported quantity -> bounded interpretation.
- **strongest rival:** An apparently different conclusion may arise from a different outcome or sample rather than a substantive contradiction.
- **discriminating observation:** Inspect original question, denominator, omitted projects and assignment procedure before comparing headline results.
- **evidence sought found:** Compared case-level claims DIG-K18, MED-K21, MOB-K21, ENE-K08, PSY-K12. Each cited case preserves a concrete definition or design qualification; overnight costs omit financing and completed samples omit cancellation, while hearing-aid price assignment was sequential.
- **remaining ambiguity:** Purposive checks do not estimate the bibliography’s error rate or invalidate the bounded observations.
- **hypothesis timing:** retrospective synthesis; targeted revision after independent first-round criticism

**Counterevidence/rival:** An apparently different conclusion may arise from a different outcome or sample rather than a substantive contradiction.

**Inference limit:** Purposive checks do not estimate the bibliography’s error rate or invalidate the bounded observations.

## SYN-K18

The acquired digital evidence leaves a universal portability-versus-performance ranking and the claim that most laptops cost less than most smartphones unestablished. It supports dated task/use comparisons and a bounded UK VR gaming minority-use result.

**Cases:** DIG-C03; DIG-C04; DIG-C06. **Evidence:** synthesis-inference. **Confidence:** medium. **Causal status:** descriptive.

Supports: Bounded evidence in DIG-K24: Constrained access is a rival to pure preference.

- [DIG-S15: Adults’ Media Literacy Core Survey 2023 Data Tables](https://www.ofcom.org.uk/siteassets/resources/documents/research-and-data/data/statistics/2024/adults-media-literacy-tracker/adults-media-literacy-core-survey-2023-data-tables?v=330749), Table 70 M 6A, pp.292–294; unweighted base 3351. Supports: Bounded evidence in DIG-K09: Combined agreement percentage and question.
- [DIG-S16: Adults’ Media Literacy Research 2023 Technical Report](https://www.ofcom.org.uk/siteassets/resources/documents/research-and-data/data/statistics/2024/adults-media-literacy-tracker/adults-media-literacy-tracker-2023-technical-report?v=330751), pp.7–8. Supports: Bounded evidence in DIG-K09: Adult weighting, sample and interview methods.
- [DIG-S15: Adults’ Media Literacy Core Survey 2023 Data Tables](https://www.ofcom.org.uk/siteassets/resources/documents/research-and-data/data/statistics/2024/adults-media-literacy-tracker/adults-media-literacy-core-survey-2023-data-tables?v=330749), Table 74 G 1 pp.307–308; fieldwork header. Supports: Bounded evidence in DIG-K18: Matched device question, levels and 3, 643 base.

**Diagnostic test:**

- **status:** underdetermined
- **mechanism chain:** Bundled device/service attributes and access -> observed use; the data do not isolate a dominant attribute.
